\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i:\,\gamma_{i}\in D\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\Bigg[\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{i}}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{i})}{\widehat{\lambda}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{i})}\Big)-\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{i}}^{r}(\gamma_{i})}{\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\gamma_{i})}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg[\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r^{\prime}_{j}}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{j})}{\widehat{\lambda}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{j})}\Big)-\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{j}}^{r}(\gamma_{j})}{\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\gamma_{j})}\Big)\Bigg].

By definition, the first sum is exactly the remainder term
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{sel}}
in the lemma.

For the single flipped event, use predictability: at time
\gamma_{j}
, the pre-
t_{j}
histories under
r
and
r^{\prime}
coincide, so
\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{j})=\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\gamma_{j})
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, and likewise
\widehat{\lambda}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{j})=\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\gamma_{j})
. Therefore the bracket simplifies to

\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r^{\prime}_{j}}^{\,r}(\gamma_{j};\theta)}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{j}}^{\,r}(\gamma_{j};\theta)}\Big)\;=\;\sigma_{j}\,\log\!\Big(\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{\,r}(\gamma_{j};\theta)}{\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{\,r}(\gamma_{j};\theta)}\Big)\;=\;\sigma_{j}\,\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\zeta;r),

where
\sigma_{j}=+1
for a
0\to 1
flip and
\sigma_{j}=-1
for a
1\to 0
flip (as defined in the lemma), and
\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\zeta;r)
is the log-ratio score at event
\zeta
.

Combining Step 1 and Step 2 yields the claimed per-flip identity.

On the window envelope event
\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)
from Assumption
S4.2
(and hence on the master event of Lemma
S4.46
), the denominators in
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}
are bounded below by
\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
by Assumption
S4.2
, while the numerators are controlled by the single flip locality bounds in Assumption
S4.2
. A direct computation gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}K_{\rm win},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B_{2}}{2}.

Therefore, on
\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)
(and hence on the master event of Lemma
S4.46
), the drift terms satisfy

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\leq\frac{2B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}K_{\rm win},\qquad\mathcal{D}_{2}\leq\frac{B_{2}}{2}.

(20)

Using
|\log a-\log b|\leq|a-b|/\min\{a,b\}
, Assumption
S4.2
, and
|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{sel}_{r}}|\leq|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r}|+|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r}|
at event times, we have the pathwise domination

|\mathcal{R}_{\rm sel}|\ \leq\ \int_{D}h(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}N(\tau),\qquad h(\tau):=\frac{|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\tau)|}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r}(\tau)|+|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r}(\tau)|}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}.

Since
h
depends only on past labels through
\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}
, it is
\mathcal{G}
-predictable under Assumption
S4.2
. Decompose

\int_{D}h\,\mathrm{d}N=\underbrace{\int_{D}h\,\mathrm{d}M^{\star}}_{=:\ \mathcal{M}_{\rm sel}}+\underbrace{\int_{D}h(\tau)\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau}_{=:\ \mathcal{D}_{\rm sel}}.

On
\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)
,
\|\lambda^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq K_{\rm win}
, hence

|\mathcal{D}_{\rm sel}|\ \leq\ K_{\rm win}\int_{D}h\ \leq\ \left(\frac{B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{B_{1}^{\Sigma}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}\right)K_{\rm win}.

Moreover,
\|h\|_{\infty}\leq H_{\rm sel}
with

H_{\rm sel}:=\frac{B_{\infty}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{2B_{\infty}^{\rm comp}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}.

Applying
Lemma

S4.34
to
\mathcal{M}_{\rm sel}
gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}\ +\ \frac{B_{\infty}^{2}}{9\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}^{2}}z_{\rm Fr}^{2}}\ +\ \frac{B_{\infty}}{3\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}z_{\rm Fr}\ \leq\ \sqrt{\frac{2B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}\ +\ \frac{2B_{\infty}}{3\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}z_{\rm Fr},

(21)

where the final inequality uses
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
, producing an additional
(B_{\infty}/(3\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}))z_{\rm Fr}
term and hence the coefficient
2/3
on the linear-in-
z_{\rm Fr}
part.

Similarly,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{\rm sel}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2K_{\rm win}\!\left(2\frac{B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+4\frac{B_{2}^{\Sigma}\,\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}^{2}}\right)z_{\rm Fr}\ +\ \frac{H_{\rm sel}^{2}}{9}z_{\rm Fr}^{2}}\ +\ \frac{H_{\rm sel}}{3}z_{\rm Fr}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\left(\frac{B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+2\frac{B_{2}^{\Sigma}\,\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}^{2}}\right)K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}\ +\ \frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{B_{\infty}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{2B_{\infty}^{\rm comp}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}\right)z_{\rm Fr}.

(22)

Consequently, setting

C_{\rm dr,0}:=\frac{B_{2}}{2},\qquad C_{\rm dr,1}:=\frac{3B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{B_{1}^{\Sigma}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}},

and

C_{\rm mtg,1}:=\sqrt{\frac{2B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}}+2\sqrt{\frac{B_{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+2\frac{B_{2}^{\Sigma}\,\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}^{2}}},\qquad C_{\rm mtg,2}:=\frac{4}{3}\!\left(\frac{B_{\infty}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}+\frac{B_{\infty}^{\rm comp}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}\right),

we have the uniform per-flip bound

\Big|\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{[u_{m}]}(\theta)-\ell_{r}^{[u_{m}]}(\theta)-\sigma_{j}\,\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\zeta;r)\Big|\ \leq\ C_{\rm dr,0}\ +\ C_{\rm dr,1}K_{\rm win}\ +\ C_{\rm mtg,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}\ +\ C_{\rm mtg,2}z_{\rm Fr}.

(23)

Hence, if the flip at
\zeta\in S_{\theta}^{\pm}(b)
creates a minority, then

\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{[u_{m}]}(r^{\prime},\theta)-\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{[u_{m}]}(r,\theta)\ \leq\ (C_{\rm dr,1}-\alpha)K_{\rm win}\ +\ \Big(C_{\rm dr,0}+C_{\rm mtg,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+C_{\rm mtg,2}z_{\rm Fr}-(b-\Delta_{s})\Big).

On
E^{\rm align}_{|D|}
, Assumption
S4.2
implies that for
\zeta\in S_{\theta}^{+}(b)
we have
\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\zeta;r)\geq b-\Delta_{s}
, and for
\zeta\in S_{\theta}^{-}(b)
we have
\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\zeta;r)\leq-b+\Delta_{s}
.If it eliminates a minority, then

\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{[u_{m}]}(r^{\prime},\theta)-\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{[u_{m}]}(r,\theta)\ \geq\ (\alpha-C_{\rm dr,1})K_{\rm win}\ -\ \big(C_{\rm dr,0}+C_{\rm mtg,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+C_{\rm mtg,2}z_{\rm Fr}\big)\ +\ (b-\Delta_{s}).

Therefore the following sufficient condition ensures elimination and non-creation of minorities on
S_{\theta}^{\pm}(b)
:

\displaystyle(\alpha-C_{\rm dr,1})K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ C_{\rm mtg,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+C_{\rm mtg,2}z_{\rm Fr}+\kappa_{b},\qquad b\geq\Delta_{s}+C_{\rm dr,0}-\kappa_{b},

(24)

for some
\kappa_{b}\in[0,C_{\rm dr,0}]
, where
\Delta_{s}=\Delta_{s}(|D|)
is the alignment slack from Assumption
S4.2
, written in shorthand on
E^{\rm align}_{|D|}
; it is independent of
b
. Note that the greedy scan (
S4.4
) eliminates minorities on decisive sets under the weaker condition
b+\alpha K_{\rm win}\geq\Delta_{s}
(Lemma
S4.53
); (
24
) is tailored to per-flip comparisons of
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{[u_{m}]}
that control future effects.

Remark S4.52

(Penalty scaling)
.

On the master event,
K_{\rm win}=O(\log|D|)
by Assumption
S4.2
, and with fixed parameter dimension we have
z_{\rm Fr}=\Theta(\log|D|)
by (
12
). Hence the right-hand side of (
24
) is of order
\log|D|
(up to polylog factors through
K_{\rm win}
). The effective penalty in (
16
) is
(\alpha-C_{\rm dr,1})K_{\rm win}
, so whenever
K_{\rm win}
grows at least on the order of
\log|D|
(the typical feedback regime captured by
C_{\rm loc}>0
in
Section

S4.2
), there exists a finite
\alpha_{0}>C_{\rm dr,1}
(depending only on the model constants and on the asymptotic ratio
z_{\rm Fr}/K_{\rm win}
) such that any fixed
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
makes

(\alpha-C_{\rm dr,1})K_{\rm win}\geq C_{\rm mtg,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+C_{\rm mtg,2}z_{\rm Fr}+\kappa_{b}

for all sufficiently large
|D|
. In other words, in this common regime
\alpha
does not need to grow with
|D|
.

Lemma S4.53

(Greedy E-step eliminates minorities)
.

Work on the event
E^{\rm align}_{|D|}
of Assumption
S4.2
. Suppose moreover that

b+\alpha K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ \Delta_{s}\qquad\text{(equivalently, }\alpha K_{\rm win}\geq\Delta_{s}-b\text{)}.

(25)

If ties in (
S4.4
) at decisive events are broken in favor of the non-minority label, then at update
m
the greedy E-step rule (
S4.4
) assigns no minority labels among events in the processed block
D_{m+1}
:
for every event index
i
with
u_{m}<t_{i}\leq u_{m+1}
,

\gamma_{i}\in S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{+}(b)\ \Rightarrow\ r_{i}^{(m+1)}=1,\qquad\gamma_{i}\in S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{-}(b)\ \Rightarrow\ r_{i}^{(m+1)}=0.

Proof S4.54
.

Fix an event index
i
with
u_{m}<t_{i}\leq u_{m+1}
and
\gamma_{i}\in S^{+}_{\theta^{(m)}}(b)
. The minority label is
0
, hence the greedy score difference between choosing
1
and choosing
0
equals

\Big(\log\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r^{(m+1)}}(\gamma_{i};\theta^{(m)})\Big)-\Big(\log\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r^{(m+1)}}(\gamma_{i};\theta^{(m)})-\alpha K_{\rm win}\Big)=\tilde{s}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\gamma_{i};r^{(m+1)})+\alpha K_{\rm win}.

By Assumption
S4.2
,
\tilde{s}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\gamma_{i};r^{(m+1)})\geq b-\Delta_{s}
, hence

\tilde{s}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\gamma_{i};r^{(m+1)})+\alpha K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ b-\Delta_{s}+\alpha K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ 0

by (
25
). Therefore the non-minority label is chosen (ties broken toward the non-minority label). If
\gamma_{i}\in S^{-}_{\theta^{(m)}}(b)
then
\tilde{s}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\gamma_{i};r^{(m+1)})\leq-b+\Delta_{s}
, so

(-\tilde{s}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\gamma_{i};r^{(m+1)}))+\alpha K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ b-\Delta_{s}+\alpha K_{\rm win}\ \geq\ 0,

and the greedy rule chooses label
0
(ties broken toward the non-minority label). Hence no minorities are assigned on

S^{+}_{\theta^{(m)}}(b)\cup S^{-}_{\theta^{(m)}}(b)
within the processed block.

Remark S4.55

(Interpretation under non-revisiting labels)
.

Under Assumption
S4.2
(b) labels are never revised, so Lemma
S4.53
only asserts that the greedy rule assigns no minority labels among the newly processed events in
D_{m+1}
relative to the current parameter
\theta^{(m)}
. Previously assigned labels (in earlier blocks) may become minority relative to later iterates
\theta^{(\ell)}
, but the analysis does not require re-elimination: it controls the blockwise mislabelling increments and propagates them to prefix error via the averaging identity in Lemma
S4.66
.

S4.5
Decisive-set stability and E-step Hamming bound

Lemma S4.56

(Decisive-set stability on the next processed block)
.

Let
B_{\theta}
be as in
Definition

S4.24
. Fix
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
and consider the next block

D_{m+1}=(u_{m},u_{m+1}]\times\mathcal{S}
.
On
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}\cap E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
(with
E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
including the compensator deviation for

B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1}
as in Assumption
S4.2
) we have

N(B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1})\ \leq\ \frac{L_{s}}{b}\,|D_{m+1}|\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ +\ N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big)\ +\ 2C_{\rm it}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}|D_{m+1}|\log|D|}.

Proof S4.57
.

Let
\Delta_{\rm LLR}:=s_{\theta^{\star}}^{\rm or}-s_{\theta^{(m)}}^{\rm or}
.
The symmetric-difference inclusions yield the pointwise bound on
D
:

\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\theta^{(m)}}}\ \leq\ \mathbbm{1}_{A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{\rm LLR}|\geq b\}}.

Intersect with
W
and multiply by
\lambda^{\star}
to obtain

\int_{B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap W}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\ \leq\ \int_{A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap W}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\ +\ \frac{1}{b}\int_{W}|\Delta_{\rm LLR}(\tau)|\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.

On
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}
, Assumption
S4.2
(applied to the subwindow
W
) gives

\int_{W}|\Delta_{\rm LLR}(\tau)|\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\ \leq\ L_{s}\,|W|\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|.

On
E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
(strengthened to include the restricted sets) we have the compensator deviations

N(B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap W)\ \leq\ \int_{B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap W}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\ +\ C_{\rm it}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}|W|\log|D|},

N(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap W)\ \geq\ \int_{A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap W}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\ -\ C_{\rm it}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}|W|\log|D|}.

Combine the last three displays to conclude.

Proposition S4.58

(E-step Hamming bound (block-normalized increment))
.

On the master event of
Lemma

S4.46
(strengthened if needed by the deterministic block/prefix restrictions
described in Assumption
S4.2
), for any update index
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
and

r^{(m+1)}
produced by the greedy E-step (
S4.4
), the block increment

\Delta_{m+1}:=d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}\!\big(r^{(m+1)},r^{\star}\big)-d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}\!\big(r^{(m)},r^{\star}\big)

satisfies

\frac{\Delta_{m+1}}{|D_{m+1}|}\ \leq\ \eta_{+}\big(|D_{m+1}|;b\big)+\eta_{-}\big(|D_{m+1}|;b\big)\ +\ \frac{L_{s}}{b}\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ +\ \frac{2}{|D_{m+1}|}N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big)\ +\ \delta_{m},

where

\delta_{m}\ :=\ C_{\rm H,1}\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}\,z_{\rm Fr}}{|D_{m+1}|}},\qquad C_{\rm H,1}:=6C_{\rm it}.

Proof S4.59
.

Work on the master event of
Lemma

S4.46
, so in particular on

E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}\cap E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
(with the deterministic subwindow restrictions included as in
Assumption
S4.2
), and assume
Lemma

S4.53
applies to the greedy E-step on the processed block
D_{m+1}
.

By Assumption
S4.2
(b), labels are not revised:
r^{(m+1)}_{i}=r^{(m)}_{i}
for all
i
with
t_{i}\leq u_{m}
.
Hence
\Delta_{m+1}
counts disagreements between
r^{(m+1)}
and
r^{\star}
only among events in
D_{m+1}
.

Define the sets (as subsets of
D
)

R_{1}:=S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{+}(b)\cap S_{\theta^{\star}}^{+}(b),\quad R_{2}:=S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{-}(b)\cap S_{\theta^{\star}}^{-}(b),\quad R_{3}:=B_{\theta^{(m)}},\quad R_{4}:=A_{\theta^{\star}}(b).

By
Lemma

S4.53
, within
D_{m+1}
all decisive-set labels match the non-minority choice at
\theta^{(m)}
.
Therefore any disagreement within
D_{m+1}
must occur either as an oracle minority on
R_{1}
/
R_{2}
,
or in
B_{\theta^{(m)}}
, or in the ambiguous band
A_{\theta^{\star}}(b)
, hence

\Delta_{m+1}\ \leq\ N_{0}(R_{1}\cap D_{m+1})+N_{1}(R_{2}\cap D_{m+1})+N(R_{3}\cap D_{m+1})+N(R_{4}\cap D_{m+1}).

Since
R_{1}\subseteq S_{\theta^{\star}}^{+}(b)
and
R_{2}\subseteq S_{\theta^{\star}}^{-}(b)
,

N_{0}(R_{1}\cap D_{m+1})\leq N_{0}\!\big(S_{\theta^{\star}}^{+}(b)\cap D_{m+1}\big),\qquad N_{1}(R_{2}\cap D_{m+1})\leq N_{1}\!\big(S_{\theta^{\star}}^{-}(b)\cap D_{m+1}\big).

Also
R_{3}\cap D_{m+1}\subseteq B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1}
and

R_{4}\cap D_{m+1}\subseteq A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}
, hence

N(R_{3}\cap D_{m+1})\leq N(B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1}),\qquad N(R_{4}\cap D_{m+1})\leq N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big).

Bound the oracle-minority terms using Assumption
S4.2
applied on the block window
D_{m+1}
and the corresponding
mean deviations included in
E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
:

\frac{N_{0}(S_{\theta^{\star}}^{+}(b)\cap D_{m+1})+N_{1}(S_{\theta^{\star}}^{-}(b)\cap D_{m+1})}{|D_{m+1}|}\ \leq\ \eta_{+}(|D_{m+1}|;b)+\eta_{-}(|D_{m+1}|;b)\ +\ 4C_{\rm it}\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}\log|D|}{|D_{m+1}|}}.

For the
B_{\theta^{(m)}}
term, apply Lemma
S4.56
with
W=D_{m+1}
and divide by
|D_{m+1}|
:

\frac{N(B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1})}{|D_{m+1}|}\ \leq\ \frac{L_{s}}{b}\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ +\ \frac{1}{|D_{m+1}|}N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big)\ +\ 2C_{\rm it}\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}\log|D|}{|D_{m+1}|}}.

Combine the last three displays and use
\log|D|\leq z_{\rm Fr}
for all large
|D|
to obtain the stated bound
with
\delta_{m}=C_{\rm H,1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}/|D_{m+1}|}
and
C_{\rm H,1}=6C_{\rm it}
.

S4.6
M-step bound via tightened label-to-score Lipschitz

Lemma S4.60

(Strong concavity: gradient inequality)
.

Let
\Theta\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
be convex and let
f:\Theta\to\mathbb{R}
be differentiable and
m_{\rm sc}
–strongly concave on

\Theta
. Then for any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta
,

\big(\nabla f(\theta^{\prime})-\nabla f(\theta)\big)^{\top}(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\ \leq\ -m_{\rm sc}\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|^{2}.

Proof S4.61
.

Strong concavity of
f
is equivalent to
m_{\rm sc}
–strong convexity of
-f
. Hence for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta
,

\big(\nabla(-f)(\theta^{\prime})-\nabla(-f)(\theta)\big)^{\top}(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)\ \geq\ m_{\rm sc}\|\theta^{\prime}-\theta\|^{2}.

Multiplying by
-1
gives the claim.

Proposition S4.62

(M-step parameter bound)
.

On the master event of
Lemma

S4.46
and under
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2
(applied to the cumulative window
D^{(m+1)}
),
for any update index
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
and
r=r^{(m+1)}
, with any maximiser

\hat{\theta}_{r}^{[u_{m+1}]}\ \in\ \arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm sc}}\ell_{r}^{[u_{m+1}]}(\theta),

we have

\left\lVert\hat{\theta}_{r}^{[u_{m+1}]}-\theta^{\star}\right\rVert\ \leq\ \underbrace{\frac{\bar{C}_{0}K_{\rm win}+\bar{C}_{1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\bar{C}_{2}z_{\rm Fr}}{m_{\rm sc}}}_{=:B_{\rm main}}\frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}(r,r^{\star})}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\ +\ \underbrace{\frac{C_{\rm sc}}{m_{\rm sc}}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(m+1)}|}}+\frac{z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\right)}_{=:\xi_{|D^{(m+1)}|}}.

In particular, under
Section

S4.2
we have
K_{\rm win}=O(\log|D|)
, and for any sequence of update indices
with
|D^{(m+1)}|\to\infty
we have
\xi_{|D^{(m+1)}|}\to 0
.

Proof S4.63
.

Let
u:=u_{m+1}
and write
\hat{\theta}:=\hat{\theta}_{r}^{[u]}
for brevity.
Since
\hat{\theta},\theta^{\star}\in\Theta_{\rm sc}
and (on the master event)
f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}
is
m_{\rm sc}
–strongly concave on
\Theta_{\rm sc}

(Assumption
S4.2
, applied to the restricted objective on
D^{[u]}
), apply
Lemma

S4.60
on
\Theta=\Theta_{\rm sc}
with

f=f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}
,
\theta=\theta^{\star}
and
\theta^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}
to get

-m_{\rm sc}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|^{2}\ \geq\ \big(\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}).

Rearranging,

m_{\rm sc}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|^{2}\ \leq\ \big(\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}).

Insert and subtract
\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})
:

\displaystyle m_{\rm sc}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star})+\big(\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}).

By Assumption
S4.2
applied to the restricted objective
f_{r}^{[u]}
with
\theta=\theta^{\star}
, we have

(\theta^{\star}-\hat{\theta})^{\top}\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\leq 0
, i.e.
(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star})^{\top}\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\geq 0
. Hence

\big(\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star})\leq\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star})\leq\|\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})\|\,\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|.

Also,

\big(\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\big)^{\top}(\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star})\leq\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\|\,\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|.

Dividing by
m_{\rm sc}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|
(or arguing trivially if
\hat{\theta}=\theta^{\star}
) yields

m_{\rm sc}\|\hat{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\|+\|\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})\|.

On the master event, the first norm is bounded by Assumption
S4.2
, and the second by Assumption
S4.2
(b) (applied on the prefix window
D^{[u]}
):

\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\hat{\theta})\|\ \leq\ \frac{d_{H}^{[u]}(r,r^{\star})}{|D^{[u]}|}\Big(\bar{C}_{0}K_{\rm win}+\bar{C}_{1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\bar{C}_{2}z_{\rm Fr}\Big),

\|\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})\|\ \leq\ C_{\rm sc}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{[u]}|}}+\frac{z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{[u]}|}\right).

Divide by
m_{\rm sc}
and note
|D^{[u]}|=|D^{(m+1)}|
.

S4.7
Two-line recursion and contraction to a statistical floor

Lemma S4.64

(Invariant local neighborhood)
.

Fix
r_{\rm sc}>0
and let
\Theta_{\rm sc}:=\Theta_{\circ}\cap B(\theta^{\star},r_{\rm sc})
.
Assume that on an event
E
the iterate sequence satisfies, for all
m<\mathfrak{T}
,

\|\theta^{(m+1)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ \leq\ \rho\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|+s,\qquad\text{with }0\leq\rho<1,\ s\geq 0.

If additionally
\|\theta^{(0)}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq r_{\rm sc}
and
s\leq(1-\rho)\,r_{\rm sc}
, then on
E
,

\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ \leq\ r_{\rm sc}\qquad\forall m\leq\mathfrak{T},

so in particular
\theta^{(m)}\in\Theta_{\rm sc}
for all
m\leq\mathfrak{T}
.

Proof S4.65
.

By induction. The base case is
\|\theta^{(0)}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq r_{\rm sc}
.
If
\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq r_{\rm sc}
then

\|\theta^{(m+1)}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq\rho\,r_{\rm sc}+s\leq\rho\,r_{\rm sc}+(1-\rho)\,r_{\rm sc}=r_{\rm sc}.

Lemma S4.66

(Averaging identity for cumulative Hamming error)
.

For each
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
define the block increment

\Delta_{m+1}:=d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}\!\big(r^{(m+1)},r^{\star}\big)-d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}\!\big(r^{(m)},r^{\star}\big),

which counts disagreements between
r^{(m+1)}
and
r^{\star}
among events in the new block

D_{m+1}=(u_{m},u_{m+1}]\times\mathcal{S}
.

Define the prefix mislabel rates by
e_{0}:=0
and, for
m\geq 1
,

e_{m}:=\frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}\!\big(r^{(m)},r^{\star}\big)}{|D^{(m)}|}.

Define the block mislabel rate
\varepsilon_{m+1}:=\Delta_{m+1}/|D_{m+1}|
.
Then for every
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
we have the exact identity

e_{m+1}=\omega_{m}\,e_{m}+(1-\omega_{m})\,\varepsilon_{m+1},\qquad\omega_{m}:=\frac{|D^{(m)}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\in[0,1),

(with
\omega_{0}=0
since
|D^{(0)}|=0
).

Proof S4.67
.

Since labels are not revised (Assumption
S4.2
(b)), we have

d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}=d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}+\Delta_{m+1}
for every
m
.
Divide by
|D^{(m+1)}|=|D^{(m)}|+|D_{m+1}|
to obtain

\frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}}{|D^{(m+1)}|}=\frac{|D^{(m)}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\cdot\frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}}{|D^{(m)}|}+\frac{|D_{m+1}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\cdot\frac{\Delta_{m+1}}{|D_{m+1}|}.

For
m\geq 1
this is exactly the claimed identity with
e_{m}=d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}/|D^{(m)}|
.
For
m=0
the same formula holds by the convention
e_{0}:=0
and
\omega_{0}=|D^{(0)}|/|D^{(1)}|=0
.

Lemma S4.68

(When localised and unlocalised M-steps coincide)
.

Let
\Theta_{\circ}\subset\Theta
be compact and convex, and let
\Theta_{\rm loc}\subseteq\Theta_{\circ}
be nonempty
with
\theta^{\star}\in\Theta_{\rm loc}
. Fix
u\in[t^{\ast},T]
and
\Delta_{\rm gap}>0
and assume the oracle gap on the
restricted objective:

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}\setminus\Theta_{\rm loc}}f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)\ \leq\ f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\Delta_{\rm gap}.

(26)

Let
r
be any labelling (possibly random) such that on an event
E_{\rm gap}^{[u]}
,

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\big|f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)-f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)\big|\ \leq\ \frac{\Delta_{\rm gap}}{4}.

(27)

Then on
E_{\rm gap}^{[u]}
every maximiser of
f_{r}^{[u]}
over
\Theta_{\circ}
lies in
\Theta_{\rm loc}
, and moreover

\arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\ =\ \arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta).

Equivalently, the localised and unlocalised restricted M-steps coincide:

\arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\ell_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\ =\ \arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm loc}}\ell_{r}^{[u]}(\theta).

Proof S4.69
.

Fix
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}\setminus\Theta_{\rm loc}
. On
E_{\rm gap}^{[u]}
,

f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\ \leq\ f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)+\frac{\Delta_{\rm gap}}{4}\ \leq\ f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\Delta_{\rm gap}+\frac{\Delta_{\rm gap}}{4}\ =\ f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\frac{3}{4}\Delta_{\rm gap}.

Also on
E_{\rm gap}^{[u]}
,

f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})\ \geq\ f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\frac{\Delta_{\rm gap}}{4}.

Therefore

f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\ \leq\ f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta^{\star})-\frac{\Delta_{\rm gap}}{2},

so no maximiser of
f_{r}^{[u]}
over
\Theta_{\circ}
can lie in
\Theta_{\circ}\setminus\Theta_{\rm loc}
.
Since
\Theta_{\rm loc}\subseteq\Theta_{\circ}
, the maximiser sets over
\Theta_{\circ}
and
\Theta_{\rm loc}
coincide.

Theorem S4.70

(Recursion and contraction to a statistical floor)
.

Assume
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2
.
For the E-step, run the greedy update (
S4.4
). Pick any
b>0
such that Assumption
S4.2
holds at that threshold, and choose
\alpha
and
\kappa_{b}\in[0,C_{\rm dr,0}]
so that (
24
) holds.
Set

A:=\frac{L_{s}}{b},\qquad B_{\rm main}:=\frac{\bar{C}_{0}K_{\rm win}+\bar{C}_{1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\bar{C}_{2}z_{\rm Fr}}{m_{\rm sc}}.

Assume additionally that the deterministic schedule satisfies the uniform growth bound

\omega\ :=\ \max_{m\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\ \frac{|D^{(m)}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\ <\ 1.

(28)

(For instance, a dyadic/doubling schedule has
\omega\approx\tfrac{1}{2}
for all
m
.)

Define the cumulative prefix mislabel rates

e_{m}\ :=\ \frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}\!\big(r^{(m)},r^{\star}\big)}{|D^{(m)}|}\qquad(m=1,\dots,\mathfrak{T}),\qquad e_{0}:=0,

and define, for each block
D_{m+1}=(u_{m},u_{m+1}]\times\mathcal{S}
, the block mislabel rate

\varepsilon_{m+1}\ :=\ \frac{\Delta_{m+1}}{|D_{m+1}|}\ =\ \frac{d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}\!\big(r^{(m+1)},r^{\star}\big)-d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}\!\big(r^{(m)},r^{\star}\big)}{|D_{m+1}|}.

Finally define the (blockwise) statistical terms

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{b,m}\ :=\ \eta_{+}(|D_{m+1}|;b)+\eta_{-}(|D_{m+1}|;b)\ +\ \frac{2}{|D_{m+1}|}N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big),

\displaystyle\delta_{m}\ :=

\displaystyle\ C_{\rm H,1}\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D_{m+1}|}},

and the (prefix) M-step noise level

\xi_{m}\ :=\ \frac{C_{\rm sc}}{m_{\rm sc}}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(m)}|}}+\frac{z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(m)}|}\right)\qquad(m=1,\dots,\mathfrak{T}),

with
C_{\rm H,1}=6C_{\rm it}
.

On the same high-probability event as in
Lemma

S4.46
, strengthened if needed by a finite union over the
deterministic family of windows
\{D^{(m)}\}_{m\leq\mathfrak{T}}
and blocks
\{D_{m}\}_{m\leq\mathfrak{T}}
for the fixed-set concentration
events invoked in
Section

S4.2
, the following hold simultaneously:

(i)

(Block error bound.)
For every
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
,

\varepsilon_{m+1}\ \leq\ A\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ +\ \varepsilon_{b,m}\ +\ \delta_{m}.

(29)

(ii)

(Prefix averaging recursion.)
For every
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
,

e_{m+1}\ \leq\ \omega_{m}\,e_{m}\ +\ (1-\omega_{m})\,\varepsilon_{m+1},\qquad\omega_{m}:=\frac{|D^{(m)}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}\ \leq\ \omega,

(30)

and hence, combining with (
29
),

e_{m+1}\ \leq\ \rho_{m}\,e_{m}\ +\ (1-\omega_{m})\big(A\,\xi_{m}+\varepsilon_{b,m}+\delta_{m}\big)\qquad(m\geq 1),

(31)

where

\rho_{m}:=\omega_{m}+(1-\omega_{m})AB_{\rm main},\qquad\rho:=\max_{0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\rho_{j}.

Note that if
AB_{\rm main}\leq 1
(in particular whenever
\rho<1
), then
\rho=\omega+(1-\omega)AB_{\rm main}
and
\rho_{m}\leq\rho
. (The base block
m=0
is initialized by (
29
) with
\theta^{(0)}
.)

(iii)

(M-step bound on prefixes.)
For every
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
,

\|\theta^{(m+1)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ \leq\ B_{\rm main}\,e_{m+1}\ +\ \xi_{m+1}.

(32)

In particular, if
\rho<1
then for every
m\in\{1,\dots,\mathfrak{T}\}
,

e_{m}\ \leq\ \rho^{m-1}e_{1}\ +\ \frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq m-1}\big(A\,\xi_{j}+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\delta_{j}\big)}{1-\rho},\qquad\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ \leq\ B_{\rm main}\,e_{m}+\xi_{m}.

Thus
\theta^{(\mathfrak{T})}
contracts to a data-dependent statistical floor. Passing from this finite-sample statement to consistency requires the additional array-level compatibility conditions isolated in Proposition
5.1
.

Proof S4.71

(Proof of
Theorem

S4.70
)
.

Work on the stated high-probability event. Fix
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
. The quantity
\Delta_{m+1}
counts mislabels in the block
D_{m+1}
produced by the greedy
rule (
S4.4
) at parameter
\theta^{(m)}
.
Proposition
S4.58
gives (
29
) directly (with
\varepsilon_{m+1}=\Delta_{m+1}/|D_{m+1}|
). Identity (
30
) is exactly Lemma
S4.66
written in terms of
(e_{m})
and
(\varepsilon_{m+1})
.
Combining (
30
) with (
29
) gives

e_{m+1}\ \leq\ \omega_{m}e_{m}+(1-\omega_{m})\Big(A\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|+\varepsilon_{b,m}+\delta_{m}\Big).

For
m\geq 1
, apply Proposition
S4.62
at index
m-1
to bound

\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\leq B_{\rm main}e_{m}+\xi_{m}
, yielding (
31
).

Equation (
32
) is exactly Proposition
S4.62
rewritten with
e_{m+1}=d_{H}^{[u_{m+1}]}/|D^{(m+1)}|
. Finally, if
\rho<1
, iterating (
31
) (with
\rho_{m}\leq\rho
) gives the displayed geometric bound on
e_{m}
,
and plugging into (
32
) yields the bound on
\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|
.
The final consistency statement follows by combining the displayed bound on
e_{m}
with (
32
): under

B_{\rm main}\,\rho^{\mathfrak{T}-1}e_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0,\qquad B_{\rm main}\,\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\big(A\,\xi_{j}+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\delta_{j}\big)}{1-\rho}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0,\qquad\xi_{\mathfrak{T}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0,

we obtain
\|\theta^{(\mathfrak{T})}-\theta^{\star}\|\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0
.

Remark S4.72

(Array-level sufficient conditions)
.

In the terminal conclusion of
Theorem

S4.70
, the quantities that must vanish are exactly

B_{\rm main}\,\rho^{\mathfrak{T}-1}e_{1},\qquad B_{\rm main}\,\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\big(A\,\xi_{j}+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\delta_{j}\big)}{1-\rho},\qquad\xi_{\mathfrak{T}}.

A convenient sufficient regime along the triangular array is

\sup_{|D|\ \text{large}}\rho\leq\bar{\rho}<1,\qquad B_{\rm main}\,e_{1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad B_{\rm main}\,\max_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\big(A\,\xi_{j}+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\delta_{j}\big)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0,\qquad\xi_{\mathfrak{T}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0,

because then
1-\rho\geq 1-\bar{\rho}>0
and, since
\mathfrak{T}\asymp\log|D|
, also

\rho^{\mathfrak{T}-1}\leq\bar{\rho}^{\mathfrak{T}-1}\to 0
.

Using the definitions of
\xi_{j}
and
\delta_{j}
, the middle condition is implied by

B_{\rm main}\,\max_{1\leq j\leq\mathfrak{T}-1}\left[A\left(\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(j)}|}}+\frac{z_{\rm Fr}}{|D^{(j)}|}\right)+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\sqrt{\frac{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}{|D_{j+1}|}}\right]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

In particular, a bound of the form
\max_{m}\omega_{m}<1
alone does not guarantee the terminal consistency claim.

Remark S4.73

(Localized versus unlocalized M-step)
.

The recursion in
Theorem

S4.70
is proved for Algorithm
10
, i.e. with the localized M-step

\theta^{(m+1)}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm sc}}\ell_{r^{(m+1)}}^{[u_{m+1}]}(\theta).

To identify this with the ordinary unconstrained M-step over
\Theta_{\circ}
, one additionally needs the gap condition of
Lemma

S4.68
.

Remark S4.74

(How
b
enters the contraction factor)
.

The contraction factor satisfies

\rho=\omega+(1-\omega)\frac{L_{s}}{b}\,B_{\rm main}.

Thus taking
b
larger improves contraction. This is only a contraction-oriented choice, however. A consistency choice must simultaneously control the ambiguous-band term and, in Hawkes models, dominate the alignment slack
\Delta_{s}
. Proposition
5.1
makes this joint requirement explicit. In particular, choosing
b\asymp K_{\rm win}
may help ensure
\rho<1
, but by itself it does not imply a vanishing-floor regime.

S4.8
When does the floor vanish? Parameter regimes

We give two sufficient regimes for the truncated kernel Hawkes model of §
S5.1
that do not require strict separation. In
Theorem

S4.70
, the dominant statistical floor enters through the blockwise ambiguous-band term
|D_{m+1}|^{-1}N(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1})
. By Assumption
S4.2
this concentrates around its compensator, so it suffices to control the
\lambda^{\star}
-mass of the ambiguous band on deterministic blocks. Accordingly, all bounds below hold verbatim with
D
replaced by any deterministic block
D_{m+1}
(or prefix
D^{(m)}
). Throughout,
C_{b}(\theta^{\star})
is as in
Definition

S4.31
and probabilities/expectations are under
P_{\theta^{\star}}
.

Write
\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau):=\mu_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})
and

U(\tau)\ :=\ \sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\big(G_{\bullet\ell}*N_{\ell}\big)(\tau)\ \ (\geq 0),\qquad\kappa\ :=\ \operatorname*{esssup}_{\tau\in D}\ \frac{U(\tau)}{\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau)}.

Note that
\kappa
is random through
U(\tau)
. In the truncated Hawkes setting with memory
h
and radius
R
,
on the window-count event
\Omega_{n}(h,R)
we have

U(\tau)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}(G_{\bullet\ell}*N_{\ell})(\tau)\leq G_{\max}\,N\big((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x)\big)\ \leq\ G_{\max}\,n,\qquad\tau=(t,x).

Hence, if
\underline{\mu}^{\rm base}_{\Sigma}:=\inf_{\tau\in D}\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau)>0
, then

\kappa\ \leq\ \frac{G_{\max}\,n}{\underline{\mu}^{\rm base}_{\Sigma}}\qquad\text{on }\Omega_{n}(h,R).

Under Assumption
S5.1.4
,
a_{\rm csc}u_{k}U\ \leq\ \sum_{\ell}(g_{k\ell}*N_{\ell})\ \leq\ b_{\rm csc}u_{k}U
for
k\in\{0,1\}
.

We first define the
\lambda^{\star}
-weighted law on
D
. Let

\Lambda^{\star}(D)\ :=\ \int_{D}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Define the probability measure on
D
with density proportional to
\lambda^{\star}
by

\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\star}}(A)\ :=\ \frac{1}{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}\int_{D}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\in A\}\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,\qquad A\subseteq D\ \text{measurable}.

Equivalently, for any measurable
g
,

\frac{1}{|D|}\int_{D}g(\tau)\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\ =\ \frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|}\,\mathbb{E}_{\lambda^{\star}}[g(\tau)],\qquad\tau\sim\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\star}}.

In particular,

C_{b}(\theta^{\star})=\frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|}\,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\star}}\!\big(|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)|\leq 2b\big).

Lemma S4.75

(Regime A: weak excitation)
.

Assume
Section

S5.1.4
. Suppose there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
L_{0}<\infty
such that the
\lambda^{\star}
-weighted distribution of the baseline log-odds

S_{0}(\tau):=\log\frac{\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})}{\mu_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})}

has a density bounded by
L_{0}
on
[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}]
. In particular, under
\tau\sim\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\star}}
,
the random variable
S_{0}(\tau)
admits a density
f_{S_{0},\lambda^{\star}}
on
[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}]
satisfying

\sup_{|x|\leq\epsilon_{0}}f_{S_{0},\lambda^{\star}}(x)\ \leq\ L_{0}.

Define

C_{A}^{\prime}\ :=\ \left(\frac{b_{\rm csc}u_{1}}{\underline{\mu}_{1}}+\frac{b_{\rm csc}u_{0}}{\underline{\mu}_{0}}\right)\cdot\sup_{\tau}\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau).

Then for any
b>0
with
2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa\leq\epsilon_{0}
,

C_{b}(\theta^{\star})\ \leq\ 2L_{0}\,(2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa)\,\frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|}.

The bound is most informative when
\Lambda^{\star}(D)/|D|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
(e.g. under stationary/ergodic regimes where
\Lambda^{\star}(D)/|D|\to\bar{\lambda}
in probability). In our finite-sample analysis we always have the envelope
\Lambda^{\star}(D)/|D|\leq K_{\rm win}
on
\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)
, while in typical stationary regimes
K_{\rm win}
is only a worst-case upper bound.

The trivial envelope
\Lambda^{\star}(D)/|D|\leq K_{\rm win}
on the window event (hence
K_{\rm win}=O(\log|D|)
)is a worst-case bound and should be viewed only as an envelope, not as the typical scaling. It follows that

C_{b}(\theta^{\star})\ \leq\ 2L_{0}\,(2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa)\,K_{\rm win}\qquad\text{on }\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R).

Thus
C_{b}(\theta^{\star})
can be made small by taking
b=b(|D|)\downarrow 0
and ensuring
\kappa
is small (e.g. on a high-probability envelope event), trading off against the band-mass term.

Proof S4.76
.

Under
Section

S5.1.4
and the bounds
a_{\rm csc}u_{k}U\leq\sum_{\ell}(g_{k\ell}*N_{\ell})\leq b_{\rm csc}u_{k}U
, we obtain

\log\left(\frac{\mu_{1}+a_{\rm csc}u_{1}U}{\mu_{0}+b_{\rm csc}u_{0}U}\right)\ \leq\ s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}\ \leq\ \log\left(\frac{\mu_{1}+b_{\rm csc}u_{1}U}{\mu_{0}+a_{\rm csc}u_{0}U}\right).

Write
s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}-S_{0}=\log(1+\delta_{1})-\log(1+\delta_{0})
with

\delta_{1}=\frac{c_{1}u_{1}U}{\mu_{1}},\ \delta_{0}=\frac{c_{0}u_{0}U}{\mu_{0}},

where
c_{1},c_{0}\in[a_{\rm csc},b_{\rm csc}]
and
\delta_{0},\delta_{1}\geq 0
. Using
\log(1+u)\leq u
for
u\geq 0
,

\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}-S_{0}\big|\ \leq\ \log(1+\delta_{1})+\log(1+\delta_{0})\ \leq\ \delta_{1}+\delta_{0}\ \leq\ \left(\frac{b_{\rm csc}u_{1}}{\mu_{1}}+\frac{b_{\rm csc}u_{0}}{\mu_{0}}\right)U.

Since
\mu_{k}\geq\underline{\mu}_{k}
,
U\leq\kappa\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau)
, and
\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau)\leq\sup_{\tau}\mu_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(\tau)
, we get

\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}-S_{0}\big|\ \leq\ C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa.

Therefore,

\{|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}|\leq 2b\}\ \subseteq\ \{|S_{0}|\leq 2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa\}.

Multiplying by
\lambda^{\star}
, integrating, and dividing by
|D|
yields

\displaystyle C_{b}(\theta^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|D|}\int_{D}\mathbbm{1}\{|S_{0}(\tau)|\leq 2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa\}\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|}\,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\star}}\!\big(|S_{0}(\tau)|\leq 2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa\big)\ \leq\ 2L_{0}\,(2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa)\,\frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|},

whenever
2b+C_{A}^{\prime}\kappa\leq\epsilon_{0}
.

Lemma S4.77

(Regime B: dominant column bias)
.

Assume
Section

S5.1.4
and baseline comparability
c_{\mu}\leq\mu_{1}/\mu_{0}\leq C_{\mu}
as in §
S5.1.4
. Let
q:=b_{\rm csc}/a_{\rm csc}
and define the bias gap

\Delta_{\rm bias}\ :=\ \log\frac{u_{1}}{u_{0}}\ -\ \log q.

Suppose
C_{\mu}\ <\ (u_{1}/u_{0})/q
(dominant bias), and assume further that the baseline is aligned with the bias:

\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})\ \geq\ \mu_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})\qquad\text{for a.e.\ }\tau\in D,

equivalently
S_{0}(\tau)=\log(\mu_{1}/\mu_{0})\geq 0
a.e. Then for any
b>0
,

\{|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}|\leq 2b\}\ \subseteq\ \{|S_{0}|\leq 2b\}.

Consequently, if the
\lambda^{\star}
-weighted distribution of
S_{0}
has a density bounded by
L_{0}
on
[0,2b]
, then

C_{b}(\theta^{\star})\ \leq\ 2L_{0}b\,\frac{\Lambda^{\star}(D)}{|D|}\ \leq\ 2L_{0}b\,K_{\rm win}\qquad\text{on }\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R).

Proof S4.78
.

For any
\tau
, define
\phi_{\rm low}(S):=\log\frac{\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+a_{\rm csc}u_{1}S}{\mu_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+b_{\rm csc}u_{0}S}
for
S\geq 0
. Its derivative is

\phi_{\rm low}^{\prime}(S)\ =\ \frac{a_{\rm csc}u_{1}\mu_{0}\ -\ b_{\rm csc}u_{0}\mu_{1}}{(\mu_{1}+a_{\rm csc}u_{1}S)(\mu_{0}+b_{\rm csc}u_{0}S)}.

The dominant-bias condition
C_{\mu}<(u_{1}/u_{0})/q
implies
\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{0}}<\frac{a_{\rm csc}u_{1}}{b_{\rm csc}u_{0}}
, so the numerator
a_{\rm csc}u_{1}\mu_{0}-b_{\rm csc}u_{0}\mu_{1}
is strictly positive. Thus
\phi_{\rm low}
is strictly increasing in
S\geq 0
, with
\phi_{\rm low}(0)=S_{0}(\tau)
and
\lim_{S\to\infty}\phi_{\rm low}(S)=\log(a_{\rm csc}u_{1}/b_{\rm csc}u_{0})=\Delta_{\rm bias}
.

By the bracketing inequality from
Lemma

S4.75
, we have
s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)\geq\phi_{\rm low}(U(\tau))
. Since
\phi_{\rm low}
is increasing,
\phi_{\rm low}(U)\geq\phi_{\rm low}(0)=S_{0}(\tau)
. Under the alignment assumption,
S_{0}(\tau)\geq 0
, hence

s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)\ \geq\ S_{0}(\tau)\ \geq\ 0,

so
|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)|=s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)
. Therefore, if
|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)|\leq 2b
then

0\ \leq\ S_{0}(\tau)\ \leq\ s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)\ \leq\ 2b,

which implies
|S_{0}(\tau)|\leq 2b
. This proves the inclusion. The bound on
C_{b}(\theta^{\star})
follows by integrating
\mathbbm{1}\{|S_{0}|\leq 2b\}
against
\lambda^{\star}
and using the density bound.

Remark S4.79

(Necessity of alignment for Regime B)
.

If the baseline favored label 0 (so
S_{0}\ll-2b
) while the feedback bias favored label 1 (so
\Delta_{\rm bias}>2b
), then the score trajectory could cross zero. In the crossing region one may have
|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}|\leq 2b
even though
|S_{0}|>2b
, invalidating the inclusion in Lemma
S4.77
. The alignment condition rules out this cancellation mechanism.

Strict separation,
\inf_{\tau}|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)|>b
, is sufficient but much stronger than necessary. Lemmas
S4.75
–
S4.77
show that it suffices to make the
\lambda^{\star}
-mass of the ambiguous band small; the algorithmic penalties then eliminate minorities on decisive sets, and the blockwise recursion of
Theorem

S4.70
handles the rest.

Throughout, we fixed a scalar threshold
b>0
to define the decisive sets
S_{\theta}^{\pm}(b)
and the ambiguous band
A_{\theta}(b)
. A mild generalization is possible if, at a given update
m
, one uses a deterministic threshold field

b_{m}:D\to(0,\infty)
on the active block. In that case the indicators

\mathbf{1}\{s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\tau)\geq b_{m}(\tau)\}
and

\mathbf{1}\{s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{(m)}}(\tau)\leq-b_{m}(\tau)\}

remain
\mathcal{G}
-predictable, and the arguments below go through with

b_{\min}^{(m)}:=\operatorname*{essinf}_{\tau\in D}b_{m}(\tau),\qquad b_{\max}^{(m)}:=\operatorname*{esssup}_{\tau\in D}b_{m}(\tau).

Define

\displaystyle S_{\theta}^{+}\!\big(b_{m}(\cdot)\big)=

\displaystyle\{\tau:\ s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)\geq b_{m}(\tau)\}

\displaystyle S_{\theta}^{-}\!\big(b_{m}(\cdot)\big)=

\displaystyle\{\tau:\ s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)\leq-b_{m}(\tau)\}

\displaystyle A_{\theta}\!\big(b_{m}(\cdot)\big)=

\displaystyle\{\tau:\ |s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)|\leq b_{m}(\tau)\}

and let
b_{\min}^{(m)}:=\operatorname*{essinf}_{\tau}b_{m}(\tau)
and
b_{\max}^{(m)}:=\operatorname*{esssup}_{\tau}b_{m}(\tau)
.
Assumption
S4.2
holds pointwise on all of
D
(and hence in particular on
S_{\theta}^{\pm}\!\big(b_{m}(\cdot)\big)
), and the per-flip bounds of Lemma
S4.50
do not depend on
b
. Consequently, the sufficient condition (
24
) with
b
replaced by
b_{\min}^{(m)}
implies the E step elimination conclusion of Lemma
S4.53
for the sets
S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{\pm}\!\big(b_{m}(\cdot)\big)
.

The decisive set stability bound also adapts with a minimal change. Writing
\Delta_{\rm LLR}:=s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{(m)}}
, the pointwise inclusions

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{+}(b_{m})\Delta S_{\theta^{\star}}^{+}(b_{m})}\ \leq

\displaystyle\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}|\leq 2b_{m}(\tau)\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{\rm LLR}|\geq b_{m}(\tau)\}},

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{S_{\theta^{(m)}}^{-}(b_{m})\Delta S_{\theta^{\star}}^{-}(b_{m})}\ \leq

\displaystyle\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{|s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}|\leq 2b_{m}(\tau)\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\Delta_{\rm LLR}|\geq b_{m}(\tau)\}}

yield, after multiplying by
\lambda^{\star}
and integrating, the analogue of Lemma
S4.56
with
b
replaced by
b_{\min}^{(m)}
in the Lipschitz term and
A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)
replaced by
A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b_{\max}^{(m)})
. On the event
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}\cap E^{\rm mass}_{|D|}
,

N\!\big(B_{\theta^{(m)}}\cap D_{m+1}\big)\ \leq\ \frac{L_{s}}{b_{\min}^{(m)}}|D_{m+1}|\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ +\ N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b_{\max}^{(m)})\cap D_{m+1}\big)\ +\ 2C_{\rm it}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}|D_{m+1}|\log|D|}.

Accordingly, the blockwise recursion in
Theorem

S4.70
holds with
A=L_{s}/b
replaced by
A_{m}=L_{s}/b_{\min}^{(m)}
,
with
N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b)\cap D_{m+1}\big)
replaced by
N\!\big(A_{\theta^{\star}}(2b_{\max}^{(m)})\cap D_{m+1}\big)
,
and with the contraction factors read as

\rho_{m}\ =\ \omega_{m}+(1-\omega_{m})\,A_{m}\,B_{\rm main},\qquad\omega_{m}=\frac{|D^{(m)}|}{|D^{(m+1)}|}.

Thus the extension is immediate for deterministic threshold fields. Extending the argument to random/predictable threshold fields would require additional concentration assumptions for the resulting random oracle sets, so we do not pursue that generalization here.

S4.9
Causal estimands as functionals

Fix a tessellation
\{\mathcal{I}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
of
D=(t^{\ast},T]\times\mathcal{S}
with
|\mathcal{I}_{j}|>0
.
For a fixed allocation
z\in\{0,1\}^{J}
, let
N^{z}
denote the post-treatment potential process under
\mathrm{do}(Z=z)
. Under the regime-stable superposition model of Assumption
3
,

N^{z}=N_{0}^{z}+N_{1}^{z},

with predictable component intensities

\lambda_{k,\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X}),\qquad k\in\{0,1\},

and total intensity

\lambda_{\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X})=\lambda_{0,\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X})+\lambda_{1,\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X}).

Here
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{z}
is the post-treatment filtration under allocation
z
from
Section

3
. The allocation
z
may affect both component intensities; it does not select one active component cellwise. Consequently, spillover allows
N_{0}^{z}
-events to occur in treated cells and
N_{1}^{z}
-events to occur in untreated cells.

Define the interventional mean measure

\Lambda_{\theta}^{z}(B):=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\!\left[N^{z}(B)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\!\left[\int_{B}\lambda_{\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right],\qquad B\subseteq D\ \text{Borel}.

Equivalently, if one wishes to keep the conditioning from
Section

3
, one may write

\Lambda_{\theta}^{z}(B\mid\mathcal{B}_{0}):=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\!\left[N^{z}(B)\mid\mathcal{B}_{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\!\left[\int_{B}\lambda_{\theta}^{z}(\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-}^{z},\mathbf{X})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\Big|\,\mathcal{B}_{0}\right].

All causal estimands in
Section

2.2
are finite linear functionals of
z\mapsto\Lambda_{\theta}^{z}
. For example,

\psi_{j}(z)=\Lambda_{\theta}^{z^{j=1}}(\mathcal{I}_{j})-\Lambda_{\theta}^{z^{j=0}}(\mathcal{I}_{j}),\qquad\psi(z)=\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\psi_{j}(z),

and

\psi(z_{a},z_{b})=\Lambda_{\theta}^{z_{a}}(D)-\Lambda_{\theta}^{z_{b}}(D).

More generally, for a finite linear functional

F(\theta)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}c_{\ell}\Lambda_{\theta}^{z_{\ell}}(B_{\ell}),

the transfer argument below applies verbatim.

Assumption S4.80

(Intervention Lipschitz envelope)

There exists
L_{\lambda}<\infty
such that, for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\circ}
, all fixed allocations
z\in\{0,1\}^{J}
,
and all Borel
B\subseteq D
,

\big|\Lambda_{\theta}^{z}(B)-\Lambda_{\theta^{\prime}}^{z}(B)\big|\ \leq\ L_{\lambda}|B|\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|.

(33)

Proposition S4.81

(Functional transfer principle)
.

Under
Section

S4.9
and the blockwise recursion of
Theorem

S4.70
, for any finite collection

\{(c_{\ell},z_{\ell},B_{\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{M}
define the functional

F(\theta):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}c_{\ell}\Lambda_{\theta}^{z_{\ell}}(B_{\ell})
.
Then, on the same high probability event as
Theorem

S4.70
, for every update index
m\leq\mathfrak{T}
,

\big|F(\theta^{(m)})-F(\theta^{\star})\big|\ \leq\ L_{\lambda}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}|c_{\ell}||B_{\ell}|\Big)\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|.

In particular, with
e_{m}=d_{H}^{[u_{m}]}(r^{(m)},r^{\star})/|D^{(m)}|
and
\xi_{m}
as in
Theorem

S4.70
,

\big|F(\theta^{(m)})-F(\theta^{\star})\big|\ \leq\ L_{\lambda}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}|c_{\ell}||B_{\ell}|\Big)\,\big(B_{\rm main}\,e_{m}+\xi_{m}\big).

If additionally
\rho<1
in
Theorem

S4.70
, then for
m\geq 1
,

\big|F(\theta^{(m)})-F(\theta^{\star})\big|\ \leq\ L_{\lambda}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}|c_{\ell}||B_{\ell}|\Big)\left(B_{\rm main}\left(\rho^{m-1}e_{1}+\frac{\max_{1\leq j\leq m-1}\big(A\xi_{j}+\varepsilon_{b,j}+\delta_{j}\big)}{1-\rho}\right)+\xi_{m}\right).

Proof S4.82
.

By linearity and (
33
),

|F(\theta)-F(\theta^{\prime})|\leq L_{\lambda}(\sum_{\ell}|c_{\ell}||B_{\ell}|)\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|
.
Apply this with
\theta=\theta^{(m)}
and
\theta^{\prime}=\theta^{\star}
.
The bounds in terms of
(e_{m})
and
(\xi_{m})
follow directly from (
32
) in
Theorem

S4.70
.

Corollary S4.83

(Compact bounds for ITE/AITE/DAITE/TAITE/DTAITE)
.

With
L_{\lambda}
as above, the plug-in estimators at
\theta^{(m)}
satisfy

\big|\widehat{\psi}_{j}(z)-\psi_{j}(z)\big|\ \leq\ 2L_{\lambda}|\mathcal{I}_{j}|\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|,

\big|\widehat{\psi}(z)-\psi(z)\big|\ \leq\ \frac{2L_{\lambda}}{J}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}|\mathcal{I}_{j}|\Big)\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|,

\big|\widehat{\psi}(z_{a},z_{b})-\psi(z_{a},z_{b})\big|\ \leq\ 2L_{\lambda}|D|\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|,

\left|\frac{\widehat{\psi}(z_{a},z_{b})-\psi(z_{a},z_{b})}{|D|}\right|\ \leq\ 2L_{\lambda}\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|.

and, more generally, any TAITE/DTAITE formed as a finite average of cell-level contrasts inherits the AITE scaling by linearity: its plug-in error is bounded by a constant multiple of
(|D|/J)\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|
, where the constant depends only on the averaging scheme. In particular, on the event of
Theorem

S4.70
,

\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\ \leq\ B_{\rm main}\,e_{m}+\xi_{m}.

Hence, whenever
\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0
, the following plug-in consistency statements hold:

(a)

ITEs are consistent for any fixed cell, more generally whenever
\sup_{j}|\mathcal{I}_{j}|<\infty
;

(b)

AITE, and likewise TAITE/DTAITE, are consistent whenever the average cell volume is uniformly bounded, i.e.

\frac{1}{J}\sum_{j=1}^{J}|\mathcal{I}_{j}|=\frac{|D|}{J}=O(1);

(c)

for DAITE, parameter consistency alone yields consistency only for the normalized estimand
|D|^{-1}\psi(z_{a},z_{b})
; the unnormalized DAITE additionally requires

|D|\,\|\theta^{(m)}-\theta^{\star}\|\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

S5
Verification for parametric families

S5.1
Specialization: truncated-kernel Hawkes

This section verifies the model-dependent parts of
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2
for Hawkes models with nonnegative kernels of finite temporal memory
h
and finite spatial range
R
. Further, it derives
Section

S4.2
from the standing gradient-level regularity part of
Section

S4.2
. Predictability of the analysed blockwise operator is enforced algorithmically (see
Section

S4.2
and Lemma
S4.43
in
Section

S4.4
). The value-level part of
Section

S4.2
is verified explicitly below; the gradient-level part of
Section

S4.2
(bounds on
\Delta(\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\lambda})
,
S_{\infty}
, and
H_{\infty}
) is used through
Lemmas

S4.36
,
S4.39
and
S5.17
and should either be checked separately from the smoothness of
\mu_{k}
and the kernels or retained as an additional standing regularity assumption. Assumption
S4.2
remains a separate model-dependent high-signal/concentration requirement; §
S4.8
only gives sufficient regimes for small ambiguous-band mass and does not constitute a full verification of
Section

S4.2
. For convenience:

•

Activity and tails:
Section

S4.2
(Proposition
S5.2
).

•

Window envelope:
Section

S4.2
(Lemma
S5.6
and Proposition
S5.7
).

•

Uniform margins and value-level single-flip locality:
Section

S4.2
and the value-level part of
Section

S4.2
(Lemmas
S5.5
and
S5.4
).

•

Alignment and LLR Lipschitz:
Sections

S4.2
and
S4.2
(see §
S5.1.4
and
Lemma

S5.15
).

•

Label-to-score Lipschitz:
Section

S4.2
from
Section

S4.2
via Proposition
S5.17
.

S5.1.1
Model and parameter space

For
k\in\{0,1\}
, the
\mathcal{G}
-predictable Hawkes process intensities are

\lambda_{k}(\tau\mid\mathcal{G}_{t-};\theta)=\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta)\,\mathrm{d}N_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime}),\qquad\tau=(t,x),

where each
g_{k\ell}(\cdot;\theta)\geq 0
is jointly measurable, supported on
(0,h]\times B_{R}(0)
, and satisfies

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\|g_{k\ell}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\infty}<\infty,\qquad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\|g_{k\ell}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{1}}<\infty,\qquad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\|g_{k\ell}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{2}}<\infty.

The truncated exponential family

g_{k\ell}(t,x;\theta)=\alpha_{k\ell}e^{-\beta t}w_{k\ell}(x)\,\mathbf{1}_{(0,h]}(t)\,\mathbf{1}_{B_{R}(0)}(x)

is a special case.

Under a candidate labelling
r
, the label-induced intensities (built from
\widehat{N}_{\ell}^{r}
) take the explicit form

\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)=\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{N}_{\ell}^{r}(\tau^{\prime}).

Assume a compact parameter neighborhood
\Theta_{\circ}
with

\displaystyle 0\leq\alpha_{k\ell}\leq\alpha_{\max},\qquad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ},\ \tau\in D}\ \mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)\leq\overline{\mu}_{k}

\displaystyle\inf_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ},\ \tau\in D}\ \mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)\ \geq\ \underline{\mu}_{k}>0

for
k\in\{0,1\}
and write
M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}:=\overline{\mu}_{0}+\overline{\mu}_{1}
. Further assume fixed
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is bounded with finite Lebesgue measure
\mu
, and
B_{R}(x)
denotes the Euclidean ball of radius
R
.

We work throughout conditional on an observed history that covers the memory window (e.g.
(t^{\ast}-h,t^{\ast}]\times\mathcal{S}
for temporal support
h
), in the same sense as §
S4.1
. For notational convenience we often specialize to the empty-history case

N\big((t^{\ast}-h,t^{\ast}]\times\mathcal{S}\big)=0.

(Hpre)

In this case the pseudo-baseline defined below reduces to
\tilde{\mu}_{k}=\mu_{k}
.
All bounds used in the Hawkes process verification extend verbatim to a conditioned start, provided the conditioned history covers the memory window. The analysis extends immediately to the case where the process is observed on a history interval
[0,t^{\ast})
(or
(-\infty,t^{\ast})
) and we maximise the likelihood conditioned on this history.

In this setting, the intensity on
D
admits the decomposition

\lambda_{k}(\tau)=\underbrace{\left(\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{(-\infty,t^{\ast})}g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\mathrm{d}N_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})\right)}_{=:\ \tilde{\mu}_{k}(\tau;\theta)}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{[t^{\ast},t)}g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\mathrm{d}N_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime}).

The term
\tilde{\mu}_{k}
acts as a spatially varying, predictable pseudo-baseline. Since the pre-
t^{\ast}
events are fixed observations, they do not participate in the E-step labelling updates.
Furthermore, the activity bounds in Proposition
S5.7
rely on domination by a stationary process
\overline{N}^{\rm stat}
(which possesses a history on
(-\infty,t^{\ast}]
), so the window envelopes
K_{\rm win}
and concentration results remain valid without modification under a conditioned start.

We write
\mathcal{B}_{R}:=B_{R}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let

G_{\bullet\ell}:=g_{0\ell}+g_{1\ell},\qquad\Gamma_{\ell}:=\int_{0}^{h}\!\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}G_{\bullet\ell}(t,x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad G_{\max}:=\max_{\ell}\|G_{\bullet\ell}\|_{\infty}.

Assume the column
L^{1}
masses
\Gamma_{\ell}
are finite.

Assumption S5.1

(Hawkes subcriticality)

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}
have entries
A_{k\ell}=\int_{0}^{h}\!\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}g_{k\ell}(t,x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\mathrm{d}t
. Assume the spectral radius
\xi(A)<1
.

S5.1.2
Predictability of the analysed operator (verifies
Section

S4.2
)

Assumption
S4.2
is enforced by the analyzed operator (Algorithm
10
) and is therefore model-agnostic.
In particular, Lemma
S4.43
shows that the blockwise greedy E-step assigns labels non-anticipatively (eventwise
\mathcal{G}_{t_{i}}
-measurability on each processed block), and the M-step at block end
u_{m}
produces
\theta^{(m)}
that is
\mathcal{G}_{u_{m}}
-measurable since it depends only on the restriction of the data to the cumulative window
D^{(m)}=(t^{\ast},u_{m}]\times\mathcal{S}
.
Thus, in the Hawkes process setting no additional model-specific verification of Assumption
S4.2
is required.
We next verify the model-dependent activity/tail condition of Assumption
S4.2
.

Proposition S5.2

(Activity and exponential tails for
N(D)
)
.

Consider the truncated kernel Hawkes model of
Section

S5.1
on
D=(t^{\ast},T]\times\mathcal{S}
, with
g_{k\ell}\geq 0
supported in
(0,h]\times B_{R}(0)
and bounded baselines
\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta^{\star})\leq\overline{\mu}_{k}
for
k\in\{0,1\}
. Assume Hawkes process subcriticality Assumption
S5.1.1
, i.e. the reproduction matrix
A
satisfies
\xi(A)<1
. Further assume the empty pre-history condition (
Hpre
). Then there exist constants
C_{\rm act}<\infty
,
\eta_{\rm act}\in(0,1)
and
c>0
(depending only on the Hawkes process parameters and on
\Theta_{\circ}
, but not on
D
) such that for all sufficiently large
|D|
,

\mathbb{E}N(D)\ \leq\ C_{\rm act}\,|D|,\qquad\mathbb{P}\!\big(N(D)\geq(1+\eta_{\rm act})C_{\rm act}|D|\big)\ \leq\ e^{-c|D|}.

In particular, Assumption
S4.2
holds.

Proof S5.3
.

Because
g_{k\ell}\geq 0
and
\xi(A)<1
, the multivariate Hawkes process admits the standard Poisson cluster (branching) representation on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}
(see, e.g., the stability/cluster constructions used already in Proposition
S5.7
). In this representation, events arise from immigrants generated by an inhomogeneous Poisson process with intensity
\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta^{\star})
for each mark
k
, and offspring generated recursively, wherein a parent of mark
\ell
produces children of mark
k
according to an inhomogeneous Poisson process with intensity kernel
g_{k\ell}
shifted to the parent location.

Let
N^{\rm imm}(D)
denote the number of immigrants in
D
(all marks). Since

\mu_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})\leq\overline{\mu}_{0}+\overline{\mu}_{1}=:M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}
pointwise,

N^{\rm imm}(D)\ \preceq\ \mathrm{Pois}(M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}\,|D|),\qquad\mathbb{E}N^{\rm imm}(D)\ \leq\ M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}\,|D|.

For
k\in\{0,1\}
, let
Z^{(k)}
be the total progeny size (all marks, including the ancestor) of a
cluster started by a single immigrant of mark
k
, in the unrestricted process (no truncation by
D
).
Under
\xi(A)<1
and compactly supported kernels,
(Z^{(k)})
has finite mean and admits a nontrivial
exponential moment: there exists
\theta_{0}>0
such that

m_{1}\ :=\ \max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{E}Z^{(k)}\ <\ \infty,\qquad m_{\theta}\ :=\ \max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{\theta Z^{(k)}}\big]\ <\ \infty\quad\text{for all }\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}].

(Heuristically: the offspring counts are Poisson with means
A_{k\ell}
, and subcriticality implies the
associated multitype Galton–Watson total population has a finite exponential moment for small
\theta>0
.)

Now dominate
N(D)
by the total number of points generated by immigrants in
D
:
if
(Z_{i})
are i.i.d. copies of a random variable
Z
satisfying
Z\overset{d}{\geq}Z^{(k)}
for both
k

(e.g. take
Z
to be a mixture maximising the mgf), then

N(D)\ \leq\ \sum_{i=1}^{N^{\rm imm}(D)}Z_{i}\qquad\text{a.s.}

Taking expectations gives

\mathbb{E}N(D)\ \leq\ \mathbb{E}N^{\rm imm}(D)\,\mathbb{E}Z\ \leq\ M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}\,m_{1}\,|D|.

Thus Assumption
S4.2
holds with
C_{\rm act}:=M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}m_{1}
.

For the tail bound, use the compound-Poisson mgf: for any
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[e^{\theta N(D)}\big]\ \leq

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\!\left[\exp\!\left(\theta\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\rm imm}(D)}Z_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle\ \exp\!\Big(\mathbb{E}N^{\rm imm}(D)\,(\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta Z}]-1)\Big)

\displaystyle\ \leq

\displaystyle\ \exp\!\big(M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}|D|\,(m_{\theta}-1)\big).

A Chernoff bound yields, for any
x>0
,

\mathbb{P}\!\big(N(D)\geq x|D|\big)\ \leq\ \exp\!\Big(-|D|\big(\theta x-M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(m_{\theta}-1)\big)\Big).

Since
x\mapsto\sup_{\theta\in(0,\theta_{0}]}(\theta x-M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}(m_{\theta}-1))
is strictly positive for
all
x
above the mean rate, there exist
\eta_{\rm act}\in(0,1)
and
c>0
such that taking

x=(1+\eta_{\rm act})C_{\rm act}
gives the claimed
e^{-c|D|}
bound for all large
|D|
.

S5.1.3
Margins, single-flip locality, and window envelopes (verifies
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2
)

Lemma
S5.4
provides explicit single-flip influence bounds for
\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r}
(and componentwise analogues), verifying the value-level part of Assumption
S4.2
. Lemma
S5.5
yields the uniform lower margins required in Assumption
S4.2
. Lemma
S5.6
gives the deterministic envelope on
\Omega_{n}(h,R)
and Proposition
S5.7
supplies the polynomial tail bound for
\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c}
, together establishing Assumption
S4.2
.

Lemma S5.4

(Single-flip influence constants)
.

Let
G_{\bullet\ell}(\cdot;\theta):=g_{0\ell}(\cdot;\theta)+g_{1\ell}(\cdot;\theta)
. For a single flip at
\zeta
,

\int_{D}|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r}|\mathrm{d}\tau\ \leq\ \|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{1}},

\int_{D}\frac{(\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r})^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \leq\ \frac{\|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}},\qquad\|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r}\|_{\infty}\ \leq\ \|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\infty}.

Analogous componentwise inequalities hold with
G_{\bullet,1}-G_{\bullet,0}
replaced by
g_{k1}-g_{k0}
. In particular, Assumption
S4.2
holds with

B_{1}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{1}},

B_{2}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\frac{\|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma}},\qquad B_{\infty}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\|G_{\bullet,1}(\cdot;\theta)-G_{\bullet,0}(\cdot;\theta)\|_{\infty},

and similarly for
B_{1}^{\rm comp},B_{2}^{\rm comp},B_{\infty}^{\rm comp}
.

Lemma S5.5

(Per-flip componentwise margin)
.

Assume
g_{k\ell}\geq 0
and
\inf_{\tau\in D}\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)\geq\underline{\mu}_{k}>0
for
k\in\{0,1\}
. For any single flip and
u\in[0,1]
, almost everywhere on
D
,

\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\tau)+u\Delta\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\tau)\ \geq\ \mu_{0}(\tau;\theta)+\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta)\ \geq\ \underline{\mu}_{0}+\underline{\mu}_{1}=:\underline{\mu}_{\Sigma},

and

\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau)+u\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau)\ \geq\ \mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)\ \geq\ \underline{\mu}_{k}\qquad(k=0,1).

Lemma S5.6

(Window envelope for Hawkes)
.

Let
L=h
and
R
as in the kernel support. On the event
\Omega_{n}(h,R)
and under (
Hpre
),

\sup_{\tau\in D}\ (\lambda_{0}^{\star}+\lambda_{1}^{\star})(\tau)\ \leq\ M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}+G_{\max}n,\qquad\sup_{\tau\in D}\ (\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r}+\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r})(\tau;\theta)\ \leq\ M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}+G_{\max}n

for all
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
and all
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm path}^{\rm flip}
. Hence
Section

S4.2
holds with
C_{\rm base}=M_{\Sigma}^{\rm base}
and
C_{\rm loc}=G_{\max}
.

Proposition S5.7

(Uniform window occupancy tail on the observation window)
.

Consider the truncated kernel Hawkes model of
Section

S5.1
on the observation window

D:=(t^{\ast},T]\times\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

with
K=2
marks. Assume that the interaction kernels
g_{k\ell}(t,x)\geq 0
are supported in
(0,h]\times B_{R}(0)
; the baseline intensities are uniformly bounded:

\sup_{k,t,x}\mu_{k}(t,x)<\infty,

and that Assumption
S5.1.1
holds, i.e. the reproduction matrix
A
satisfies
\xi(A)<1
.

Let
|D|:=(T-t^{\ast})\,\mu(\mathcal{S})
denote the spacetime volume of
D
, and for
n\in\mathbb{N}
define

\Omega_{n}(h,R):=\Bigl\{\sup_{(t,x)\in D}N\bigl((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)\leq n\Bigr\}.

For technical convenience, also define the closed-window variant

\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}(h,R):=\Bigl\{\sup_{(t,x)\in D}N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)\leq n\Bigr\}.

Since
N((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x))\leq N([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x))
, we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}(h,R)\subseteq\Omega_{n}(h,R)
and hence
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n}(h,R)^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}(h,R)^{c})
.

Then for every
\eta>0
there exist constants
c_{1}(\eta),c_{2}(\eta)>0
(depending only on the model parameters, on
h,R
and on
\eta
) such that,
with

n_{*}(|D|):=\bigl\lceil c_{1}(\eta)\,\log|D|\bigr\rceil,

we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c}\bigr)\;\leq\;c_{2}(\eta)\,|D|^{-\eta}.

Proof S5.8
.

Throughout the proof, constants may change from line to line but depend only on the Hawkes-process parameters, on
h,R
, and on the geometry of
\mathcal{S}
, not on
D
. Let
N_{k}
be the marked counting measure for mark
k\in\{0,1\}
, and let

N:=N_{0}+N_{1}

be the total (unmarked) counting measure, so that for any Borel set
B\subset D
we have
N(B)=N_{0}(B)+N_{1}(B)
.

For each
k\in\{0,1\}
, define the spatially aggregated (ground) process

\widetilde{N}_{k}(I):=N_{k}(I\times\mathcal{S}),\qquad I\subset\mathbb{R}\ \text{Borel},

and set

\widetilde{N}(I):=\widetilde{N}_{0}(I)+\widetilde{N}_{1}(I).

Clearly, for any
(t,x)\in D
,

N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)\;\leq\;\widetilde{N}\bigl([t-h,t]\bigr).

(34)

Let
\lambda_{k}(t,x)
denote the intensity of
N_{k}
at
(t,x)
, and let
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t)
be the intensity of
\widetilde{N}_{k}
:

\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\lambda_{k}(t,x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x).

By the model definition and Fubini,

\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\mu_{k}(t,x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{(t-h,t)}\!\!\int_{\mathcal{S}}\!\int_{\mathcal{S}}g_{k\ell}(t-s,x-y)\,N_{\ell}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x).

For each pair
(k,\ell)
and lag
u>0
, define the envelope kernel

\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}(u):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{k\ell}(u,z)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(z),

and note that for any
y\in\mathcal{S}
,

\int_{\mathcal{S}}g_{k\ell}(u,x-y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\;\leq\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{k\ell}(u,z)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(z)\;=\;\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}(u).

Thus, using
\mu(\mathcal{S})<\infty
, set

\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}:=\mu(\mathcal{S})\,\sup_{t\in[t^{\ast},T],\,x\in\mathcal{S}}\mu_{k}(t,x)\ <\ \infty.

Then

\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t)\;\leq\;\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{(t-h,t)}\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}(t-s)\,\widetilde{N}_{\ell}(\mathrm{d}s).

Define the purely temporal
2
-dimensional linear Hawkes process
\overline{N}=(\overline{N}_{0},\overline{N}_{1})
on
\mathbb{R}
with baselines
\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}
and interaction kernels
\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}
, i.e.

\overline{\lambda}_{k}(t)=\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{(t-h,t)}\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}(t-s)\,\overline{N}_{\ell}(\mathrm{d}s).

Its reproduction matrix is exactly
A=(A_{k\ell})
as in Assumption
S5.1.1
:

A_{k\ell}=\int_{0}^{h}\varphi^{\mathrm{env}}_{k\ell}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u=\int_{0}^{h}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{k\ell}(u,z)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(z)\,\mathrm{d}u.

Let
(\Pi_{k})_{k\in\{0,1\}}
be independent Poisson random measures on
[t^{\ast},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
with intensity
dt\,dz
. We construct
\overline{N}
by the standard Poisson embedding for (linear) Hawkes processes with nonnegative kernels; see, e.g.,
(
9
, Proposition 14.7.I)
,
3
, and
4
:

\overline{N}_{k}(dt):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq\overline{\lambda}_{k}(t-)\}}\,\Pi_{k}(dt,dz),\qquad\overline{\lambda}_{k}(t)=\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}+\sum_{\ell}\int\varphi^{\rm env}_{k\ell}(t-s)\,\overline{N}_{\ell}(ds).

On the same Poisson basis, define
\widetilde{N}
analogously by

\widetilde{N}_{k}(dt):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t-)\}}\,\Pi_{k}(dt,dz),

which is the coupled construction used in
(
3
, Lemma 3)
.

Define the first-violation time

\tau:=\inf\Bigl\{t\geq t^{\ast}:\exists k\text{ with }\widetilde{N}_{k}([t^{\ast},t])>\overline{N}_{k}([t^{\ast},t])\Bigr\}.

On
\{\tau>t\}
we have
\widetilde{N}_{\ell}|_{[t^{\ast},t)}\leq\overline{N}_{\ell}|_{[t^{\ast},t)}
for all
\ell
, and hence, by the nonnegativity of
\varphi^{\rm env}
(monotonicity of the linear Hawkes process map),

\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}(t-)\leq\overline{\mu}_{k}^{\rm agg}+\sum_{\ell}\int\varphi^{\rm env}_{k\ell}(t-s)\,\widetilde{N}_{\ell}(ds)\leq\overline{\mu}_{k}+\sum_{\ell}\int\varphi^{\rm env}_{k\ell}(t-s)\,\overline{N}_{\ell}(ds)=\overline{\lambda}_{k}(t-).

Therefore the acceptance region for
\widetilde{N}_{k}
is contained in that of
\overline{N}_{k}
at time
t
(cf. the monotone coupling argument in
3
), so
\tau=\infty
almost surely and
\widetilde{N}\leq\overline{N}
pathwise. In particular,

\widetilde{N}_{k}(J)\leq\overline{N}_{k}(J)\quad\text{for all Borel intervals }J\subset[t^{\ast},\infty),\ \ k\in\{0,1\}.

(35)

Intuitively,
\widetilde{N}
is obtained by thinning the envelope
\overline{N}
via the common Poisson embedding
(
9
, Proposition 14.7.I)
.

Because the kernels are nonnegative and
\xi(A)<1
, the process
\overline{N}
admits a unique stationary version
\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}
on
\mathbb{R}

(
3
)
. Using the standard Poisson cluster representation, we may construct the empty-history envelope
\overline{N}
on
[t^{\ast},\infty)
by removing all clusters whose ancestral point lies before
t^{\ast}
from
\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}
; hence, for any interval
J\subset[t^{\ast},\infty)
,

\overline{N}_{k}(J)\;\leq\;\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}_{k}(J)\quad\text{a.s.}

(36)

Combining (
34
),
(
35
) and (
36
), we obtain
the pathwise bound

N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R^{\prime}}(x)\bigr)\;\leq\;\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}\bigl([t-h,t]\bigr):=\sum_{k=0}^{1}\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}_{k}\bigl([t-h,t]\bigr)

(37)

for every
t\in[t^{\ast},T]
, every
x\in\mathcal{S}
, and for every radius
R^{\prime}\geq 0

(since the right-hand side does not depend on
R^{\prime}
).

We next obtain an exponential tail bound for a single temporal window. The process
\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}
is a standard multivariate linear Hawkes process with nonnegative, compactly supported kernels and
\xi(A)<1
. Therefore, we can apply
(
16
, Proposition 2)
to
\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}
on the interval
[-h,0)
: there exists
\theta>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\exp\bigl(\theta\,\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}([-h,0))\bigr)\bigr]<\infty.

By stationarity, for any interval
I
of length
h
we have

\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}(I)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}([-h,0)).

Fix such an interval
I
and set

M:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\exp\bigl(\theta\,\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}(I)\bigr)\bigr]<\infty.

A Chernoff bound then gives

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}(I)>n\bigr)\;\leq\;Me^{-\theta n}\qquad\text{for all }n\geq 0.

(38)

The constants
M
and
\theta
depend only on the Hawkes process parameters and on

h
, not on the particular choice of
I
.

It remains to discretize
D
and apply a union bound. Let
\Delta_{t}:=h/2
and define

\mathsf{T}:=\bigl\{t_{i}:=t^{\ast}+i\Delta_{t}:\ i=0,1,\dots,I_{T}\bigr\},\quad I_{T}:=\Bigl\lceil\frac{T-t^{\ast}}{\Delta_{t}}\Bigr\rceil.

Then

|\mathsf{T}|\leq 2\,(T-t^{\ast})/h+2\leq C_{t}(T-t^{\ast})/h

for some absolute constant
C_{t}
.

Because
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is bounded, there exists a finite set
\mathcal{X}=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{J}\}\subset\mathcal{S}
such that

\mathcal{S}\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{J}B_{R/2}(x_{j}).

We keep
J
implicit; it depends only on
\mathcal{S}
,
R
and the dimension
d
, and in particular does not depend on the observation window
D
.

Set

\mathcal{G}:=\mathsf{T}\times\mathcal{X},\qquad|\mathcal{G}|=|\mathsf{T}|\,|\mathcal{X}|\;\leq\;C_{t}J\,\frac{T-t^{\ast}}{h}\;=\;C_{\rm grid}\,|D|

for some constant
C_{\rm grid}>0
depending only on
h,R
and the geometry of
\mathcal{S}
(we used
|D|=(T-t^{\ast})\,\mu(\mathcal{S})
here).

For the covering/union-bound argument we work with enlarged spatial balls
B_{2R}(y)
, so that any target ball
B_{R}(x)
is contained in some
B_{2R}(y)
from the spatial grid. For each
(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}
, consider the spacetime window

W_{s,y}:=[s-h,s]\times B_{2R}(y).

By (
37
) (with radius
2R
) and (
38
), we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl(N(W_{s,y})>n\bigr)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\bigl(\overline{N}^{\mathrm{stat}}([s-h,s])>n\bigr)\;\leq\;Me^{-\theta n}

for every
(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}
and all
n\geq 0
. A union bound over the finite grid
\mathcal{G}
yields

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\max_{(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}}N(W_{s,y})>n\Bigr)\;\leq\;|\mathcal{G}|\,Me^{-\theta n}\;\leq\;C_{\rm grid}M\,|D|\,e^{-\theta n}.

(39)

For any
(t,x)\in D
, choose
s\in\mathsf{T}
and
y\in\mathcal{X}
such that

|t-s|\leq\Delta_{t}=h/2,\qquad\|x-y\|\leq R/2.

Then
B_{R}(x)\subset B_{3R/2}(y)\subset B_{2R}(y)
and

[t-h,t]\subset[s-2h,s+h].

The interval
[s-2h,s+h]
is contained in the union of three intervals of length
h
, for example
[s-2h,s-h]
,
[s-h,s]
, and
[s,s+h]
. Hence

[t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\subset\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{3}\bigl([s_{\ell}-h,s_{\ell}]\times B_{2R}(y)\bigr),

for suitable
s_{\ell}\in\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
in a time grid
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
obtained by shifting
\mathsf{T}
by multiples of
h
. Enlarging
\mathcal{G}
if necessary to

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}:=\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{X},

we obtain

N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)\;\leq\;3\max_{(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}N\bigl([s-h,s]\times B_{2R}(y)\bigr).

The cardinality of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is still bounded by a constant multiple of
|D|
:
|\mathcal{G}^{\prime}|\leq C^{\prime}_{\rm grid}|D|
for some
C^{\prime}_{\rm grid}>0
.

Taking a supremum over
(t,x)\in D
yields

\sup_{(t,x)\in D}N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)\;\leq\;3\max_{(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}N\bigl([s-h,s]\times B_{2R}(y)\bigr).

Therefore,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\sup_{(t,x)\in D}N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)>3n\right)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\!\left(\max_{(s,y)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}N\bigl([s-h,s]\times B_{2R}(y)\bigr)>n\right).

Applying (
39
) (with
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and possibly a different grid constant) gives

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\sup_{(t,x)\in D}N\bigl([t-h,t]\times B_{R}(x)\bigr)>3n\right)\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}M\,|D|\,e^{-\theta n}

for some
C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}>0
.

Finally, let
\eta>0
be arbitrary and choose

n=n_{0}(|D|):=\Bigl\lceil\frac{\eta+1}{\theta}\,\log|D|\Bigr\rceil.

Then

C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}M\,|D|\,e^{-\theta n}\;\leq\;C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}M\,|D|\,e^{-(\eta+1)\log|D|}\;=\;C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}M\,|D|^{-\eta}.

Absorbing the multiplicative constant
3
in front of
n
into the definition of
c_{1}(\eta)
(i.e. replacing
n_{0}(|D|)
by
3n_{0}(|D|)
) and renaming constants

c_{1}(\eta):=\frac{3(\eta+1)}{\theta},\qquad c_{2}(\eta):=C^{\prime\prime}_{\rm grid}M,

we obtain

n_{*}(|D|):=\bigl\lceil c_{1}(\eta)\,\log|D|\bigr\rceil

Thus we have shown
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c})\leq c_{2}(\eta)\,|D|^{-\eta}
. Since
\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c}\subseteq\widetilde{\Omega}_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c}
, the same bound holds for
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)^{c})
.

Remark S5.9

(Local strong concavity for multivariate Hawkes)
.

For multivariate Hawkes processes, uniform local strong concavity of the (complete-data) log-likelihood around the truth, including for parameterizations that contain nonlinear kernel-shape parameters, is established in
11
. The proof follows the local curvature strategy used in
16
(control of the empirical Hessian/Gram term on a fixed-radius neighborhood), and in the present spatiotemporal truncated setting the same conclusion can be obtained by combining those local curvature arguments with the same monotone coupling / window-envelope control used in our Hawkes process verification (e.g. the coupling behind Proposition
S5.7
) to transfer the needed uniform concentration bounds to the spatiotemporal observation window, verifying Assumption
S4.2
.

S5.1.4
Verification of Assumptions
S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2

Assumption S5.10

(Column similarity (sufficient for uniform alignment))

There exist deterministic numbers
\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}\geq 0
such that, uniformly over
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
,

\sup_{(t,x)\in(0,h]\times\mathcal{B}_{R}}\big|g_{k0}(t,x;\theta)-g_{k1}(t,x;\theta)\big|\ \leq\ \varepsilon_{k},\qquad k\in\{0,1\}.

Set
\varepsilon_{\rm col}:=\varepsilon_{0}+\varepsilon_{1}
.

Remark S5.11

(Checking the column-similarity constants
\varepsilon_{k}
)
.

For the truncated exponential parametrization

g_{k\ell}(t,x;\theta)=\alpha_{k\ell}e^{-\beta t}w_{k\ell}(x)\,\mathbf{1}_{(0,h]}(t)\,\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}(x),\qquad\mathcal{B}_{R}:=B_{R}(0),

a convenient choice in Assumption
S5.1.4
is

\varepsilon_{k}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\ \sup_{(t,x)\in(0,h]\times\mathcal{B}_{R}}\big|g_{k0}(t,x;\theta)-g_{k1}(t,x;\theta)\big|.

Since
e^{-\beta t}\leq 1
on
(0,h]
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\ \sup_{x\in\mathcal{B}_{R}}\big|\alpha_{k0}w_{k0}(x)-\alpha_{k1}w_{k1}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\Big(|\alpha_{k0}-\alpha_{k1}|\,\|w_{k0}\|_{\infty}+\alpha_{\max}\,\|w_{k0}-w_{k1}\|_{\infty}\Big),

using
0\leq\alpha_{k\ell}\leq\alpha_{\max}
on
\Theta_{\circ}
and the triangle inequality.
Thus Assumption
S5.1.4
reduces to a simple “parameter closeness” condition on

(\alpha_{k0},\alpha_{k1},w_{k0},w_{k1})
.

Proposition S5.12

(Uniform score alignment under column similarity)
.

Consider the truncated Hawkes model of
Section

S5.1
with nonnegative kernels supported on

(0,h]\times\mathcal{B}_{R}
.
Assume
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S5.1.4
.
Work either under the empty-history condition (
Hpre
) or, more generally, conditional on the
marked history of
(N_{0},N_{1})
on
(t^{\ast}-h,t^{\ast}]\times\mathcal{S}
(so that the marked past entering the Hawkes functional is fixed under the conditioning).
Work on the window-count event
\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)
from
Section

S4.2
.
Then for every
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
, every deterministic labelling
r
, and for a.e.
\tau\in D
,

\big|\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\tau;r)-s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)\big|\ \leq\ \Delta_{s},\qquad\Delta_{s}:=\frac{n_{*}(|D|)}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}\varepsilon_{\rm col}.

In particular, Assumption
S4.2
holds with

E^{\rm align}_{|D|}:=\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R),\qquad\Delta_{s}(|D|):=\frac{n_{*}(|D|)}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}\varepsilon_{\rm col}.

Moreover, since

\mathbb{P}\big((E^{\rm align}_{|D|})^{c}\big)\leq c_{2}|D|^{-\eta_{\rm win}},

after possibly decreasing the exponent and enlarging the large-
|D|
threshold, this matches the probability requirement in Assumption
S4.2
.

Proof S5.13
.

Fix
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
and a deterministic labelling
r
.
Let
r^{\star}
denote the true labelling on
D
.
Under the Hawkes model specification,

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)=

\displaystyle\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta)\,d\widehat{N}_{\ell}^{r}(\tau^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}(\tau\mid\mathcal{G}_{t-};\theta)=

\displaystyle\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta)\,dN_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime}).

Under the stated conditioning (empty history or marked pre-
t^{\ast}
history fixed),
the marked past entering the Hawkes functional is the same in
\widehat{\lambda}^{r^{\star}}
and in the oracle intensity,
and moreover
\widehat{N}_{\ell}^{r^{\star}}=N_{\ell}
on
D
.
Therefore
\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\star}}(\tau;\theta)=\lambda_{k}(\tau\mid\mathcal{G}_{t-};\theta)
for a.e.
\tau\in D
, hence

\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\tau;r^{\star})=s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)
.

Fix
\tau=(t,x)\in D
and
k\in\{0,1\}
. Because
g_{k\ell}
is supported on
(0,h]\times\mathcal{B}_{R}
,
only events in the truncated neighbourhood
(t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x)
contribute. On
\Omega_{n_{*}}(h,R)
there are at most

n_{*}(|D|)
such events.
For any event
\tau_{i}
in this neighbourhood, if
r_{i}=r_{i}^{\star}
then its contribution is the same under
r
and
r^{\star}
;
if
r_{i}\neq r_{i}^{\star}
, then its contribution changes from the column
r_{i}^{\star}
kernel to the column
r_{i}
kernel, and

Section

S5.1.4
yields a pointwise bound by
\varepsilon_{k}
.
Therefore,
