\big|\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)-\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\star}}(\tau;\theta)\big|\ \leq\ n_{*}(|D|)\,\varepsilon_{k}.

Using
\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)\geq\underline{\mu}_{k}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\star}}(\tau;\theta)\geq\underline{\mu}_{k}

from
Section

S4.2
, the mean-value bound
|\log a-\log b|\leq|a-b|/\underline{\mu}_{k}
gives

\big|\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)-\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\star}}(\tau;\theta)\big|\ \leq\ \frac{n_{*}(|D|)\varepsilon_{k}}{\underline{\mu}_{k}}\ \leq\ \frac{n_{*}(|D|)\varepsilon_{k}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}.

Finally,

\big|\tilde{s}_{\theta}(\tau;r)-s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)\big|=\Big|\big(\log\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r}-\log\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r}\big)-\big(\log\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{r^{\star}}-\log\widehat{\lambda}_{0}^{r^{\star}}\big)\Big|\leq\sum_{k=0}^{1}\big|\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}-\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\star}}\big|,

so the claim follows by summing the previous display over
k=0,1
.

Assumption S5.14

(CSC and baseline comparability)

There exist weights
u_{0},u_{1}>0
with
u_{0}+u_{1}=1
, and constants
0<a_{\rm csc}\leq b_{\rm csc}<\infty
such that, for each column
\ell
and a.e. point
(t,x)
with
G_{\bullet\ell}(t,x)>0
,

a_{\rm csc}u_{k}\ \leq\ \frac{g_{k\ell}(t,x)}{G_{\bullet\ell}(t,x)}\ \leq\ b_{\rm csc}u_{k},\qquad k\in\{0,1\}.

In addition, there exist
0<c_{\mu}\leq C_{\mu}<\infty
such that

c_{\mu}\ \leq\ \frac{\mu_{1}(\tau;\theta)}{\mu_{0}(\tau;\theta)}\ \leq\ C_{\mu}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in D,\ \theta\in\Theta_{\circ}.

All constants are uniform over
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
.

Lemma S5.15

(Lipschitz control for the oracle score (Assumption
S4.2
))
.

Let
N=(N_{0},N_{1})
be a bivariate spatio-temporal Hawkes process on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
We observe it on
D=(t^{\ast},T]\times\mathcal{S}
, where
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is bounded and
0<\mu(\mathcal{S})<\infty
.
Write
\tau=(t,x)
and
d\tau:=dt\otimes d\mu(x)
.
For
k\in\{0,1\}
, the
\mathcal{G}
-predictable conditional intensities are

\lambda_{k}(\tau;\theta)=\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int_{(t-h,t)}\int_{B_{R}(x)}g_{k\ell}(t-s,x-y;\theta)\,N_{\ell}(ds,dy),

with compactly supported bounded kernels. Denote by
\lambda^{\star}(\tau):=\lambda_{0}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+\lambda_{1}(\tau;\theta^{\star})
the true total
intensity and let

s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau):=\log\frac{\lambda_{1}(\tau;\theta)}{\lambda_{0}(\tau;\theta)}

be the oracle log-likelihood ratio.

Assume:

(A1) Uniform positivity and relative comparability.

There exist constants
0<\nu_{-}\leq\nu_{+}<\infty
and
C_{\mu}^{\rm rel},C_{g}<\infty
such that, for all

\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
, all
k,\ell
, and a.e.
(u,z)\in(0,h]\times B_{R}(0)
,

\nu_{-}\leq\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)\leq\nu_{+},\qquad\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta^{\star})\leq C_{\mu}^{\rm rel}\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta),\qquad g_{k\ell}(u,z;\theta^{\star})\leq C_{g}\,g_{k\ell}(u,z;\theta).

(A2) Uniform component-ratio bounds on
\Theta_{\circ}
.

There exist constants
0<c_{\rm rat}\leq C_{\rm rat}<\infty
such that for all
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
and

\tau\in D
,

c_{\rm rat}\ \leq\ \frac{\lambda_{1}(\tau;\theta)}{\lambda_{0}(\tau;\theta)}\ \leq\ C_{\rm rat}.

(For example, this holds under CSC + baseline comparability.)

(A3) Activity bounds on schedule enlargements.

Let the (deterministic) schedule windows be
\{D^{(m)}\}_{m=1}^{\mathfrak{T}}
and
\{D_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\mathfrak{T}}
as in §
S4.1
.
For any window
W=(a,b]\times\mathcal{S}
in

\mathcal{W}_{\rm sch}:=\{D\}\cup\{D^{(m)}:\ 1\leq m\leq\mathfrak{T}\}\cup\{D_{m}:\ 1\leq m\leq\mathfrak{T}\},

define its enlargement

W^{+}:=(a-h,b]\times\mathcal{S}^{+R},\qquad\mathcal{S}^{+R}:=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\mathrm{dist}(y,\mathcal{S})\leq R\}.

Assume there exist constants
K_{\rm act},\eta>0
such that for all large
|D|
,

\Pr\!\Big(\bigcap_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{\rm sch}}\{\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}N_{\ell}(W^{+})\leq K_{\rm act}|W^{+}|\}\Big)\ \geq\ 1-|D|^{-\eta}.

Define the event

E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}:=\bigcap_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{\rm sch}}\left\{\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}N_{\ell}(W^{+})\leq K_{\rm act}|W^{+}|\right\}.

Then Assumption
S4.2
holds: there exists a deterministic quantity
L_{s}(|D|)<\infty
such that, on

E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}
, for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\circ}
,

\int_{D}\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\prime}}(\tau)\big|\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,d\tau\leq L_{s}(|D|)\,|D|\,\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}.

(40)

The same argument applies with
D
replaced by any
W\in\mathcal{W}_{\rm sch}
, yielding the schedule-uniform bounds required in Assumption
S4.2
. Moreover,
\Pr((E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|})^{c})\leq|D|^{-\eta}
by (
S5.15
), hence Assumption
S4.2
holds with the same exponent.

Proof S5.16
.

We first develop an explicit relative-comparability constant. Under (A1), for every
\theta\in\Theta_{\circ}
, every
\tau
and every
k

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{\star}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta^{\star})+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta^{\star})\,\mathrm{d}N_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\rm rel}\Bigl(\mu_{k}(\tau;\theta)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\int g_{k\ell}(\tau-\tau^{\prime};\theta)\,\mathrm{d}N_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{\rm rel}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\theta),

where
C_{\rm rel}:=\max\{C_{\mu}^{\rm rel},C_{g}\}
. In particular,
\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\leq C_{\rm rel}\bigl(\lambda_{0}(\tau;\theta)+\lambda_{1}(\tau;\theta)\bigr)
.

Next, we find a mean-value bound for
s^{\rm or}
and removal of denominators. Fix
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\circ}
. Since

s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)=\log\lambda_{1}(\tau;\theta)-\log\lambda_{0}(\tau;\theta)
and
\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\geq\nu_{-}>0
,
the mean value theorem gives, for each
\tau
,

\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta}(\tau)-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\prime}}(\tau)\big|\leq\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}\,\sup_{\vartheta\in[\theta,\theta^{\prime}]}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\frac{\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}}{\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)}.

Multiplying by
\lambda^{\star}(\tau)
and using
\lambda^{\star}\leq C_{\rm rel}(\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1})
yields

\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\sum_{k=0}^{1}\frac{\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}}{\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)}\leq C_{\rm rel}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\frac{\lambda_{0}(\tau;\vartheta)+\lambda_{1}(\tau;\vartheta)}{\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)}\,\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}.

By the ratio bounds (A2), for all
k
,

\frac{\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{k}}=1+\frac{\lambda_{1-k}}{\lambda_{k}}\leq 1+\max\{C_{\rm rat},1/c_{\rm rat}\}=:C_{\rm mix}.

Hence, uniformly in
\vartheta\in\Theta_{\circ}
,

\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\sum_{k=0}^{1}\frac{\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}}{\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)}\leq C_{\rm rel}C_{\rm mix}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}.

Therefore,

\int_{D}\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta}-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\prime}}\big|\,\lambda^{\star}\,d\tau\leq C_{\rm rel}C_{\rm mix}\,\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}\int_{D}\sup_{\vartheta\in\Theta_{\circ}}\sum_{k=0}^{1}\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(\tau;\vartheta)\|_{2}\,d\tau.

(41)

We now obtain a linear bound on gradients in terms of local counts. Because
\Theta_{\circ}
is compact and the kernel family is truncated-exponential with bounded
w_{k\ell}
,
there exist deterministic constants
C_{0},C_{1}<\infty
(depending only on
\Theta_{\circ},h,R,w_{\infty}
and the baseline
smoothness on
\Theta_{\circ}
) such that, for all
\vartheta\in\Theta_{\circ}
and
\tau=(t,x)
,

\sum_{k=0}^{1}\|\nabla_{\theta}\lambda_{k}(t,x;\vartheta)\|_{2}\leq C_{0}+C_{1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}N_{\ell}\!\big((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x)\big).

(42)

We proceed to develop a spatio-temporal Fubini bound for the averaged local counts. Let

v_{R}:=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu(B_{R}(y))<\infty

(which equals
\mu(B_{R})
when
\mu
is Lebesgue measure). Here and below we write
D^{+}:=(t^{\ast}-h,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{+R}
, i.e.
D^{+}=W^{+}
with
W=D
. For each
\ell\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{t^{\ast}}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{S}}N_{\ell}\!\big((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x)\big)\,\mu(dx)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{(t^{\ast}-h,T)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{t^{\ast}}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{s\in(t-h,t)\}\,dt\Big)

(43)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\int_{S}\mathbf{1}\{y\in B_{R}(x)\}\,\mu(dx)\Big)\,N_{\ell}(ds,dy)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{D^{+}}h\,v_{R}\,N_{\ell}(ds,dy)=h\,v_{R}\,N_{\ell}(D^{+}),

(44)

since
\int_{t^{\ast}}^{T}\mathbf{1}\{s\in(t-h,t)\}\,dt\leq h
and

\int_{S}\mathbf{1}\{y\in B_{R}(x)\}\,\mu(dx)=\mu(\mathcal{S}\cap B_{R}(y))\leq v_{R}
,
and the integrand vanishes outside
D^{+}
. The same computation with
t
integrated over
(a,b]
(instead of
(t^{\ast},T]
) yields

\int_{a}^{b}\int_{\mathcal{S}}N_{\ell}((t-h,t)\times B_{R}(x))\,\mu(dx)\,dt\ \leq\ hv_{R}\,N_{\ell}(W^{+}),\qquad W=(a,b]\times\mathcal{S},

so on
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}
we have
N_{\ell}(W^{+})\leq K_{\rm act}|W^{+}|
uniformly over
W\in\mathcal{W}_{\rm sch}
.

We now conclude (Assumption
S4.2
) on
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}
. Combine (
41
), (
42
) and (
44
) to get

\int_{D}\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta}-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\prime}}\big|\,\lambda^{\star}\,d\tau\leq C_{\rm rel}C_{\rm mix}\,\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}\Big(C_{0}|D|+C_{1}hv_{R}\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}N_{\ell}(D^{+})\Big).

On
E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|}
we have
\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}N_{\ell}(D^{+})\leq K_{\rm act}|D^{+}|
, hence

\int_{D}\big|s^{\rm or}_{\theta}-s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\prime}}\big|\,\lambda^{\star}\,d\tau\leq C_{\rm rel}C_{\rm mix}\,\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}\Big(C_{0}|D|+C_{1}hv_{R}K_{\rm act}|D^{+}|\Big).

Define

L_{s}(|D|):=C_{\rm rel}C_{\rm mix}\left(C_{0}+C_{1}hv_{R}K_{\rm act}\,\frac{|D^{+}|}{|D|}\right),

which is deterministic given the window geometry and model constants. Then (
40
) follows.
Finally, by (
S5.15
) we have
\Pr((E^{\rm Lip}_{|D|})^{c})\leq|D|^{-\eta}
, completing the verification of Assumption
S4.2
.

Proposition S5.17

(Label-to-score Lipschitz for truncated Hawkes)
.

Assume
Sections

S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
,
S4.2
and
S4.2
.
Assume the Hawkes process kernels are supported on
(0,L]\times B_{R}(0)
and are nonnegative.
Define

V_{L,R}:=L\,\mu(\mathcal{B}_{R}),\qquad\text{so that}\qquad|(t,t+L]\times B_{R}(x)|\leq V_{L,R}\ \ \text{for all }(t,x).

Redefine (if needed)
K_{\rm win}\leftarrow K_{\rm win}\vee 1
, where
K_{\rm win}
is the deterministic envelope from

Section

S4.2
.

For an event
\gamma_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})
and a schedule endpoint
u\in\{u_{m}\}_{m\leq\mathfrak{T}}
define the forward cone on
D^{[u]}
,

C_{j}^{[u]}:=\big((t_{j},u]\cap(t_{j},t_{j}+L]\big)\times B_{R}(x_{j}).

For
z_{\rm Fr}>0
, define the cone-count event

E^{\rm cone}(z_{\rm Fr}):=\bigcap_{u\in\{u_{m}\}_{m\leq\mathfrak{T}}}\ \bigcap_{j\leq N^{[u]}}\left\{N(C_{j}^{[u]})\ \leq\ V_{L,R}\,K_{\rm win}+\sqrt{2V_{L,R}\,K_{\rm win}\,z_{\rm Fr}}+\frac{2}{3}z_{\rm Fr}\right\}.

Define the deterministic constants

L_{\log}:=\frac{S_{\infty}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}},\qquad C_{\log}:=\frac{\overline{B}_{\infty}^{\rm comp}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}+\frac{S_{\infty}\,B_{\infty}^{\rm comp}}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}^{2}},

and

\bar{C}_{0}:=V_{L,R}\,C_{\log}+2L_{\log}+\overline{B}_{1}^{\Sigma},\qquad\bar{C}_{1}:=\sqrt{2V_{L,R}}\,C_{\log},\qquad\bar{C}_{2}:=\frac{2}{3}C_{\log}.

Then on
\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)\cap E^{\rm cone}(z_{\rm Fr})
, for every update
m\in\{0,\dots,\mathfrak{T}-1\}
with
u=u_{m+1}

and
r=r^{(m+1)}
, uniformly over
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm sc}
,

\left\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)\right\|\ \leq\ \frac{d_{H}^{[u]}(r,r^{\star})}{|D^{[u]}|}\Big(\bar{C}_{0}K_{\rm win}+\bar{C}_{1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\bar{C}_{2}z_{\rm Fr}\Big).

In particular (by uniformity in
\theta
), the same bound holds at any restricted M-step maximiser

\hat{\theta}_{r}^{[u]}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\Theta_{\rm sc}}\ell_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)
, which is exactly
Section

S4.2
.

Moreover, by Lemma
S4.41
and Lemma
S4.34
, for each
z_{\rm Fr}>0
,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\big(E^{\rm cone}(z_{\rm Fr})\big)^{c}\cap\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)\cap E_{\rm act}\right)\ \leq\ (1+\eta_{\rm act})C_{\rm act}|D|\,\mathfrak{T}\,e^{-z_{\rm Fr}}.

Proof S5.18
.

Fix
u
and
\theta\in\Theta_{\rm sc}
. Let
(r^{(j)})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{I}}
be the canonical telescoping path from
r
to

r^{\star}
from
Definition

S4.22
, where
\mathfrak{I}=d_{H}^{[u]}(r,r^{\star})
and each adjacent pair
differs by a single flip at some index
i_{j}\leq N^{[u]}
. By the triangle inequality,

\big\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)\big\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\mathfrak{I}}\big\|\nabla f_{r^{(j-1)}}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r^{(j)}}^{[u]}(\theta)\big\|.

It therefore suffices to bound the change in
\nabla f^{[u]}
under a single flip.

Fix a single flip at
\gamma_{i}=(t_{i},x_{i})
and denote the two labellings before/after the flip by
r
and
r^{\prime}
.
Write
W:=D^{[u]}
for brevity. By definition,

\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)=\frac{1}{|W|}\left\{\sum_{j:\,t_{j}\leq u}\nabla_{\theta}\log\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{j}}^{r}(\gamma_{j};\theta)-\int_{W}\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\tau;\theta)\,d\tau\right\}.

With respect to the event-sum part, at the flipped event
\gamma_{i}
, predictability implies

\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{i};\theta)=\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\gamma_{i};\theta)
for
k\in\{0,1\}
, so the only change
is the component index inside
\log(\cdot)
. Hence

\left\|\nabla\log\widehat{\lambda}_{r^{\prime}_{i}}^{r^{\prime}}(\gamma_{i};\theta)-\nabla\log\widehat{\lambda}_{r_{i}}^{r}(\gamma_{i};\theta)\right\|\leq 2\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\nabla\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\gamma_{i};\theta)\|\leq 2L_{\log}.

For any other event
\gamma_{j}
with
t_{j}\leq u
, changing the label at
\gamma_{i}
can only affect

\widehat{\lambda}_{\cdot}(\gamma_{j};\theta)
if
\gamma_{j}\in C_{i}^{[u]}
(kernel support
(0,L]\times B_{R}
).
On
C_{i}^{[u]}
, for each
k
,

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\prime}}(\tau;\theta)-\nabla\log\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)\big\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\nabla\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\prime}}(\tau;\theta)}{\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r^{\prime}}(\tau;\theta)}-\frac{\nabla\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)}{\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\Delta(\nabla\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r})(\tau;\theta)\|}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}}+\frac{\|\nabla\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)\|}{\underline{\mu}_{\min}^{2}}\,|\Delta\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}(\tau;\theta)|\ \leq\ C_{\log},

by
Sections

S4.2
and
S4.2
. Therefore the event-sum contribution is bounded by

2L_{\log}+C_{\log}\,N(C_{i}^{[u]}).

With respect to the compensator part, by the
L^{1}
gradient influence bound in
Section

S4.2
,

\left\|\int_{W}\nabla\widehat{\lambda}^{r^{\prime}}(\tau;\theta)\,d\tau-\int_{W}\nabla\widehat{\lambda}^{r}(\tau;\theta)\,d\tau\right\|\leq\int_{W}\|\Delta(\nabla\widehat{\lambda}^{r})(\tau;\theta)\|\,d\tau\leq\overline{B}_{1}^{\Sigma}.

Combining (i) and (ii),

\big\|\nabla f_{r^{\prime}}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\big\|\leq\frac{1}{|W|}\Big(2L_{\log}+C_{\log}N(C_{i}^{[u]})+\overline{B}_{1}^{\Sigma}\Big).

On
E^{\rm cone}(z_{\rm Fr})
, substitute the bound on
N(C_{i}^{[u]})
and use
K_{\rm win}\geq 1
to absorb

2L_{\log}+\overline{B}_{1}^{\Sigma}
into the
K_{\rm win}
term, giving

\big\|\nabla f_{r^{\prime}}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)\big\|\leq\frac{1}{|W|}\Big(\bar{C}_{0}K_{\rm win}+\bar{C}_{1}\sqrt{K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\bar{C}_{2}z_{\rm Fr}\Big).

We now sum over the telescoping path. Summing the single-flip bound over
\mathfrak{I}=d_{H}^{[u]}(r,r^{\star})
flips yields the stated inequality for

\|\nabla f_{r}^{[u]}(\theta)-\nabla f_{r^{\star}}^{[u]}(\theta)\|
.
Uniformity in
\theta
allows evaluation at
\theta=\hat{\theta}_{r}^{[u]}
.

Fix
(u,j)
and consider
C_{j}^{[u]}
. By Lemma
S4.41
,

\mathbf{1}_{C_{j}^{[u]}}
is
\mathcal{G}
-predictable, so Freedman inequality applies to

N(C_{j}^{[u]})-\int_{C_{j}^{[u]}}\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,d\tau.

On
\Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)
we have
\|\lambda^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq K_{\rm win}
, hence

\int_{C_{j}^{[u]}}\lambda^{\star}\leq K_{\rm win}|C_{j}^{[u]}|\leq V_{L,R}K_{\rm win}
.
Therefore

\mathbb{P}\!\left(N(C_{j}^{[u]})>V_{L,R}K_{\rm win}+\sqrt{2V_{L,R}K_{\rm win}z_{\rm Fr}}+\frac{2}{3}z_{\rm Fr},\ \Omega_{n_{*}}(L,R)\right)\leq e^{-z_{\rm Fr}}.

On
E_{\rm act}
, the number of cones in the defining intersection of
E^{\rm cone}(z_{\rm Fr})
is at most

\sum_{m=1}^{\mathfrak{T}}N^{[u_{m}]}\leq\mathfrak{T}N(D)\leq(1+\eta_{\rm act})C_{\rm act}|D|\,\mathfrak{T}
, so the union bound yields
the displayed probability bound in the proposition.

S5.2
Specialization: (in)homogeneous Poisson with dispersion

We next consider the Poisson limit of Hawkes processes, i.e.
\xi(A)=0
, so that
g_{k\ell}\equiv 0
and
\lambda_{k}=\mu_{k}
. The same no-feedback simplification holds for a common predictable dispersion field
V(\tau)
in a Cox-type specification
\lambda_{k}(\tau)=V(\tau)\mu_{k}(\tau)
, provided
V
is controlled so that the bounded-intensity and concentration assumptions continue to hold. In this case there is no label feedback:
\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{r}\equiv\lambda_{k}
for every labelling
r
. Hence Assumption
S4.2
holds with zero flip-influence constants, Assumption
S4.2
holds with
\Delta_{s}=0
, and Assumption
S4.2
reduces to a bounded-intensity condition with
K_{\rm win}=O(1)
. The remaining requirements reduce to ordinary regularity conditions for the two-component Poisson family, namely positive bounded intensities, local curvature/identifiability of the Poisson log-likelihood, and the decisive-set mass condition in Assumption
S4.2
. Under those conditions, the hard-EM floor in
Theorem

S4.70
has no feedback-propagation term and is driven only by oracle-minority mass on decisive sets together with the ambiguous-band mass
C_{b}(\theta^{\star})
. Thus hard assignments may plateau at this classification floor, whereas SEM-style averaging can still remain consistent because label errors do not propagate through time.

Lemma S5.19

(Ambiguous-band mass for Poisson)
.

Let

S_{0}(\tau):=s^{\rm or}_{\theta^{\star}}(\tau)=\log\frac{\lambda_{1}(\tau\mid\mathcal{H}_{t-};\theta^{\star})}{\lambda_{0}(\tau\mid\mathcal{H}_{t-};\theta^{\star})}.

Suppose the
\lambda^{\star}
-weighted distribution of
S_{0}
has a density bounded by
L_{0}
on
[-2b,2b]
. Then

C_{b}(\theta^{\star})=\frac{1}{|D|}\int_{D}\mathbbm{1}\{|S_{0}(\tau)|\leq 2b\}\,\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\ \leq\ 4L_{0}b\,K_{\rm win}.

Proof S5.20
.

Immediate from the definition,
\lambda^{\star}(\tau)\leq K_{\rm win}
, and the density bound.
